## Points and Places

### 1. Assembly Hill
About a kilometer north of Clearwine Fort is a rocky hill overlooking the surrounding countryside. Here the free men and women of the Colymar assemble each Movement Week of Storm Season to deliberate affairs, resolve disputes between the clans, and to offer sacrifices to Orlanth Rex. The Rex statue is brought from the royal palace to Assembly Hill and sacrifices are offered for peace and victory. Several thousand people gather for the assembly. People stay with kinfolk, in inns in Clearwine Fort, or set up tents around the hill.

The assembly grounds are used throughout the year by entertainers visiting Clearwine Fort.

### 2. Small Walls
These walls were built around two centuries ago. They are only two-to-four meters high and are made of fieldstone.
Within the walls are the sacred vineyards of the Earth Temple.
Here Hareva was given the clearwine grape by the goddess.

### 3. Earth Temple and Sacred Fields
This temple is located outside of the walls of Clearwine Fort.
A major temple to Ernalda, with shrines to Asrelia, Babeester Gor, and Maran Gor, this is one of the most important Earth Temples in Dragon Pass. The temple has a shrine to Flamal as a husband-protector. It is a square building, with the inner sanctum underground. The temple’s interior is decorated with stone carvings and paintings of deities. The high priestess is always of the Ernaldoring clan, either by birth, marriage, or adoption.

The vineyards within the small walls belong to the Earth Temple and are sacred to the Ernalda cult. The rich farmlands between Clearwine Fort and The Stream also belong to the temple; the tenant farmers grow grain and wine for the cult.
Collectively, these lands are called the Sacred Fields.

### 4. Blue Mound
The decorative blue glazed tiles that once covered the stone walls of Clearwine Fort were removed as part of the ritual performed by Chief Colymar to clear out the draconic malignance. The tiles, as well as other draconic artifacts found within the fort, were broken and piled in a great burial mound. Called the Blue Mound, this is a place strong in draconic magic. Ever since the Dragonewts Dream started in 1539, dragonewts occasionally visit without comment or explanation.

### 5. City Walls
The walls of Clearwine Fort are made of massive blocks of stone and range between 4 and 12 meters in height. There is a walkway atop the walls, protected by a narrow battlement on the outer edge resembling the teeth of dragons.

### 6. Dragon’s Gate
This is the main approach into the Royal Citadel. The gate consists of two great monoliths capped with a huge lintel; the opening is roughly 3 meters tall and 3 meters wide. Above the lintel have been carved two dragons flanking a pillar (thought by some to represent Kero Fin or even the Spike).
The gate can be sealed with a pair of massive wooden doors.

### 7. Royal Citadel
The Royal Citadel is the oldest part of Clearwine Fort and its foundations date to before the Dawn. It is located on the highest part of the hill and the walls here are also the highest—ranging from 8 to 12 meters high. The citadel can be accessed from the Upper City by a ramp that leads through the King’s Gate. The royal palace and the necropolis occupy most of the interior of the citadel.

### 8. Palace of the Kings
This complex is built around the king’s audience chamber.
This rectangular hall has an open hearth that is vented through an oculus in the roof above it and surrounded by four columns. The king sits atop the magical Ivory Throne to meet with councilors, petitioners, and envoys in this chamber, and hold feasts for tribal thanes and warriors. A great bronze cauldron, a gift from Palashee Longaxe to the Colymar and large enough to hold over 400 liters of wine, is kept here for ritual celebrations.

Surrounding the audience chamber is a system of interconnected courtyards, archive rooms, workshops, living quarters, religious shrines, armories, and storerooms. The Rex Statue of the Orlanth Rex cult is kept most days in a room near the audience chamber.

The king’s household and retinue have living quarters within the palace. Most of the Royal Bodyguard reside here.

### 9. Necropolis
This is the burial ground of dead Colymar kings and their families. The dead are placed on pyres and burnt. Their cremated bones and effects are put in urns and placed underground in stone chambers, often accompanied by weapons, jewelry, and other valuables, and covered with a cairn or barrow.

The tombs have not been looted, due to their proximity to the royal palace. The necropolis is surrounded by a low stone wall.

### 10. Thunder Tower
This great round tower is made of huge blocks of stone.
It overlooks the entrance to the citadel and a secondary entrance to the Lower City. It is 25 meters tall and 20 meters in diameter. The tower is self-sufficient and may be sealed off from the rest of the city—it contains sufficient food to feed a score of people for four weeks. The tower serves as a minor temple to Orlanth Thunderous and contains barracks for twenty warriors, maintained by the king.

It is connected to the citadel by a single huge slab of stone that bridges the Main Gate into the Upper City.

### 11. Hero’s Trail
This steep and well-hidden trail leads to a sally port on the north wall. It pierces the wall at an oblique angle via a long, narrow, and unobtrusive passage through its thickness. At both ends, the passage is guarded by reinforced wooden doors, which, when opened, allow the defenders to slide through singly if the need arises.

### 12. Upper City
The buildings here are built of stone, with tiled roofs made of timber, slate, or baked clay. Buildings typically adjoin one another and open towards an inner circular street that winds its way from the Lower City to the Citadel. Most houses are the residences of wealthy families, although the district includes two inns, stables, a red-smith, and several temples (including the Lightbringers Temple). The Lightbringers Temple and the weekly market are the heart of the district and the center of city life.

### 13. Market
This plaza is the location of the weekly market. A towering, bronze statue of Issaries, the god of communication and trade, is on the south side. On the north side is the Lightbringers Temple. Jaranstand White Grape is the market priest, and he resolves disputes between merchants.

### 14. Lightbringers Temple
This building across from the market consists of a central courtyard open to the sky, surrounded by stone corridors.
There are altars for offering sacrifices to each of the gods of the temple. A chamber along the rear of the temple serves as the sanctuary where cult images and sacred regalia are kept.
Portions of the building double as a hospital.

The temple serves as a major temple to Orlanth Adventurous, with minor temples to Orlanth Thunderous and Rex, shrines to all the Lightbringers, and an associated shrine to Ernalda. Nearby are the homes of the tribal priests.

### 15. White Grape Inn
A freshly painted sign of a cluster of white grapes swings in the breeze outside this inn, one of two in Clearwine Fort.
Sora Goodseller, an Issaries priest from the Holy Country, recently purchased this inn. Travelers, merchants, and foreigners patronize the inn. This large stone building has two floors, a cellar, an attached warehouse, and stables. The main floor of the building is the tavern and common room for meals and entertainment. The upper floor is sleeping rooms for rent. The cellar contains foodstuffs and wine.
There is always at least one mercenary guard here. Prices for food, drink, and rooms are as per the RuneQuest rules, page 413.

### 16. Old Gate and Ramp
This is the original entrance to the Upper City, dating back to the First Age. It is accessed by a ramp paved with stones.
The gate can be sealed with heavy wooden doors, although they have not been for more than a century.

### 17. Lower City
This area is settled by farmers, crafters, and foreigners.
Buildings typically adjoin one another and open towards an inner circular street that winds its way to the Upper City.
There are many workshops here.

The Lower City is separated from the Upper City by a steep cliff and by the old walls of the Upper City. There are two gates leading out of the Lower City into the Sacred Fields. These gates can be closed with wooden doors.

### 18. Sartar’s Tower
King Sartar built this tower in 1500. It is of formidable craftsmanship, 31 meters tall and 15 meters wide at the base.
The tower is used as a watchtower and as a signaling beacon and manned by several archers of the king.

### 19. Temple of the Tribe
This open-air altar is where black bulls are sacrificed by tribal leaders to the wyter of the Colymar tribe, as part of the Orlanth Rex cult.

### 20. The Ram’s Head
A skeletal ram’s head with immense horns hangs above the entry to this three-story stone and timber building. Hareva Weaver, Issaries priest and counselor of the king operates the inn. The ground floor is the tavern and common room for meals and entertainment. The upper floors have both private rooms and dormitory style barracks for visitors. Prices for food, drink, and rooms as per the RuneQuest rules, page 413

### 21. Ernaldoring Hall
This is the home of the Ernaldoring chieftain and the center of his household. It is a large, square, two-story building, built last century in the Esrolian style, ordered around a large central courtyard.

# Other Places of Interest
## Alebard’s Tower
This hollow-walled drystone tower is about 20 meters in diameter and stands about 12 meters tall. Its walls are thick at the base and taper at the top. It serves as a shrine to Humakt the War God. Prior to Starbrow’s Rebellion, this was the headquarters of the Humakti Temple of the Wooden Sword.

## Apple Lane
This market hamlet is in territory disputed by the Colymar and Malani tribes. It is noteworthy for its temple to Uleria, the goddess of Love. The hamlet also has a fortified roadhouse and caravanserai—the Tin Inn—on the trade road between Runegate and Jonstown and was the home of the noted Issaries Priest Gringle Goodsale until 1621 when Lunar soldiers chased him out of Sartar and burned down his pawnshop. See Apple Lane chapter.

## Black Altar
This large black stone is used by Darkness cults for sacrifice.
It has a groove carved in it to drain blood into a stone receptacle. Trolls and other Darkness worshipers can often be found here.

## Birne’s Squeeze
The trade road between Apple Lane and Jonstown runs through the Thunder Hills by way of this narrow, winding, and rocky ravine. Travelers are often ambushed here; as a result, the phrase “Birne’s Squeeze” is used by the Colymar as a euphemism for highway robbery.

## Boulder Ruins
These ruins atop Stael’s Hills were once a complex of temples and ruins but are now little more than foundations and walls made of huge polygonal-shaped stones. The vaulted roofs of the complex collapsed centuries ago and the stones were long ago quarried. Here the Varmandi found the goddess who now resides in the fine temple at Greenstone.

## Broken Tower
This ruined tower and surrounding ruins is avoided by the local herders. See the RuneQuest Quickstart Adventure “The Broken Tower” for more information.

## Chaos Ground
This tainted place at the edge of Stael’s Hills was created when the Crimson Bat bled here in 1602. It is a place of power for Chaos-worshiping cults, and Chaotic things can occasionally be found here.

## Cinder Pits
In a Gods War battle pitting the storm gods against the sky gods, this was the place the dead gods were cast down and burned. It is fuming bowl of ash and smoke. Loud noises can sometimes be heard coming from within.

## Dragonewt Plinths
There are four sets of dragonewt plinths in the Colymar tribal lands, forming a straight line over many kilometers. Each set consists of a pair of standing stones which appear to be a crude and stylized representation of dragons, wyrms, and an otherwise unknown thing vulgarly called a “frogosaurus.” The plinths are part of a functioning dragonewt road between the dragonewt city deep in the Quivin Mountains called Highwyrm and Stones-over-Souls, a dragonewt city near Dunstop in Tarsh. As a result, dragonewts are common in

The dragonewt roads are described on pages 44–45 of the Glorantha Bestiary.

## Dragonewt Band
This band of dragonewts is currently in the Colymar lands, keeping an eye on the activities of Yerezum Storn, a recurring dream dragon that has recently reappeared in the area around Apple Lane (see __The Dragon of the Thunder Hills__, page 104).

The dragonewts will not fight unless they are attacked. As far as dragonewts go, this group will be remarkably friendly as they regularly speak to humans, looking for the humans that will perform utuma for Yerezum Storn. Once they determine that someone is not likely to perform the rite, they lose all interest in them.

To the human eye, all dragonewts of a given type look alike, even to the extent of possessing individual personalities.
If they do possess individuality, dragonewts do not display it in the presence of non-dragonewts. In dealings with others, the dragonewts follow a strict code. They will not speak to a non-dragonewt unless spoken to first. Only the minimum required answer will be given. Only Sarna Ya’qal speaks to outsiders, although Slave to Lies translates for it.

The dragonewts are not easily coerced by force. They do not fear physical death. Only spiritual destruction holds any terror for them.

#### Sarna Ya’qal (Tailed Priest)
Sarna fluctuates between extremes of honorable and dishonorable, greedy and generous, impatient and patient, and extrovert and introvert. It has been given the task of finding the correct humans to perform utuma for Yerezum Storn and is willing to make great sacrifices (including entanglements in the material world) to carry out its task.

## Dragonewt Weapons, Skills, and Magic
> Dragonewts are complex and ancient creatures, with unusual weapons specicic to their race, as well as unique practices,
particularly skills and magic. All are described at length in the Glorantha Bestiary and are summarized here for the purposes of this adventure.

### Dragonewt Weapons

Dragonewt weapons are fashioned of obsidian, stone, wood, dragonbone, or leather, and among dragonewts they are given or made, never purchased. Base chance for a human using any dragonewt weapon is 05%.

- Klanth: A weapon made with obsidian blades set into a wooden haft and grip. (Slashing)
- Korff: A slightly-curved dragonbone sword that appears to be made of ivory. Dragonewts will go to any length to recover a lost korff. (Cutting)
- Utuma: A ceremonial stone dagger used in ritual suicide. (Impaling)
- Gami: A three-pronged dragonbone sword-catcher, primary used as a parrying weapon. On a special success parry, the attacker is disarmed. On a critical success parry, the attacker’s weapon is broken, regardless of its hit points. (Cutting)
- Long Bow: A large bone bow. (Impaling)
- Sling: As per normal slings, though the pocket and loops are often made of human, troll, or dragonewt skin. (Crushing)
- Samarin and Chokin: These are small throwing weapons that can be easily concealed in the hand. Up to five can be thrown at one time, but each thrown after the first subtracts –10% from each attack. (Impaling)

### Dragonewt Skills

- Don Armor (05): This is a dragonewt-only Manipulation skill: the base 05% chance comes through a successful mystic heroquest. Successful use of the skill allows the dragonewt to don its ornamental dragonbone armor in 1D10+10 strike ranks. When the armor is put on, the gamemaster should roll D100. Each full 05% rolled (round down) gives the armor 1 point of damage absorption, to the limit of the user’s Don Armor skill. Dragonewts use this skill before important journeys or battles, and the armor is left on until the mission is accomplished.
- Quickdraw (05): This Manipulation skill lets the user draw their sword from the scabbard and attack in one smooth, uninterrupted motion. If the Quickdraw skill is used successfully, the user needn’t wait the additional 5 strike ranks before attacking once their weapon is drawn. If the skill fails, the user will be the last to attack in that round but can still parry. If the skill roll is fumbled, the user has lost their weapon and thrown it 1D6 meters in a random direction, in addition to a roll on the Fumble table.
- Worship (utuma ritual) (00): This mysterious dragonewts spiritual ritual utilizes the utuma and is never taught to outsiders. Its secrets will not be revealed here.

### Dragonewt Magic

Dragonewt magic (sometimes called “dragon magic”) is learned through esoteric contemplation and is different from Rune magic, spirit magic, or sorcery. When the dragonewt wills an effect, it takes place on SR 1. Effects are not spells—a dragonewt simply knows or does not know an effect.
- Attune Landscape: The dragonewt achieve oneness with its surroundings, becoming nearly impossible to see so long as it does not move (once it moves, the effect ends). Unless a viewer receives a special success in the appropriate Perception skill, it cannot be detected. This even applies to spells such as Second
Sight or Detect Enemies. If the dragonewt succeeds in using its Hide skill when invoking this effect, any potential enemy must be aware of the presence or possible presence of the dragonewt to even attempt using a Perception skill (and the viewer still needs a special success to spot the dragonewt).
- Soul-blast: The POW of the dragonewt is matched against that of its target via a blast of green energy.
If the dragonewt succeeds, the target loses 2D6 magic points.

#### Crested Dragonewt—Slave to Lies
This dragonewt’s jaws and tongue have been surgically altered to make it better at speaking human languages. Sarna Ya’qal uses it as a translator; the tailed priest speaks in Auld Wyrmish, Slave to Lies translates and then translates anything said by the humans.

### Gejay Hills
These hills are also called the Oakland Back and separate the Colymar from the Malani tribe. They are a mix of oak woodland and grassland. Herders and bandits populate the hills.

### Hound Knob
This stone formation is atop a rounded hill that rises above Tarndisi’s Grove. Local hunters avoid the area as a monstrous hound called Black Growler haunts it.

#### Black Growler

This demonic hound originated in the Underworld. It escaped when the Sun stopped in 373 and was bound here by an ancient curse. The Black Growler must return to the Hound Knob each dawn, reappearing each dusk to hunt. It avoids the Aldryami of Tarndisi’s Grove and the dragonewts but considers humans and other mortals to be prey.


##### Characteristics
- STR 17
- CON 31
- SIZ 17
- INT 16
- DEX 11
- POW 25
- CHA —
- Hit Points: 35
- Base SR: 4
- Move: 10
- Runes: Darkness 90%, Death 90%.
- Magic Points: 25
- Reputation: Killing the Black Growler is worth +1D6% Reputation.
- Damage Bonus: +1D6
- Armor: 3-point hide.
- Skills: Scent Prey 75%, Track 75%, Underworld Lore 90%.
- Languages: Speak Beastspeech 50%, Speak Darktongue 50%.

Howl: Black Growler can Demoralize foes that hear its howl. If the listener fails to resist POW vs. POW, they are Demoralized and attempt to flee.


### Kjartan’s Lake
This large alpine lake is situated in a boggy vale, rich with berries. To the east are the forested foothills of the Quivin Mountains.

### Larnste’s Table
See the description under __Temples__ (page 14).

### Lunar Manors

These are the ruins of fortified slave farms built by the Lunars after Starbrow’s Rebellion, and where the local clans paid their annual tribute to the Lunar Empire. The farms, their records, and their shrines were all destroyed after the Dragonrise and the overseers killed, although most of the slaves escaped. They now stand empty and ruined.

### Nymie Vale
This is a cultivated valley of small farms, vineyards, and pastures for cattle and sheep. Ruins of older civilizations are scattered around the vale.

### Odd Tower

During Starbrow’s Rebellion a part of the Lunar army made a feint across the River at Two Sisters and constructed several observation towers by the Upland Marsh. This tower is the only one to have survived from that time. It is still used as an observation post.

### Old Grave
This is the burial mound of King Berevenenos, a longforgotten Second Age hero who died fighting against the dragons. His mound is six meters high and has a diameter of nearly 20 meters. On the south side of the mound, a hole big enough for a man to crawl has been dug into the mound. A careful look with a torch will show that the hole penetrates the center of the mound. A heavy slab of rock has been removed, permitting entrance into the burial chamber.
Within is an oblong stone chamber divided into two rooms:

Outer Chamber: The adventurers enter here. On the floor are bronze and ceramic dishes that centuries ago held food and drink. There is also a desiccated corpse holding a silver goblet—the Lunar grave robber who dug the hole and tried to rob the grave goods. The bronze and ceramic dishes might be worth a grand total of 5 L if cleaned and a willing buyer is found, and the silver goblet alone is worth an additional 5 L.

Inner Chamber: On a raised stone dais is the decorated burial urn of Berevenenos. Surrounding the urn are his grave goods (see __Grave Goods__, below). Noticeable among these items are the ornate silver helmet, iron cuirass, great shield, weapons, and jewelry.

#### Ghost of King Berevenenos

If anyone enters the inner chamber, Berevenenos will manifest with righteous anger and indignation—he rages that the people of this land have forgotten him, and his resting place violated by would-be thieves. He attacks intruders unless they placate him with offers of annual gifts of food and drink. If an adventurer makes such an offer, but later fails to provide the offerings, Berevenenos will curse their clan, giving them a –25% to the Sacred Time Harvest rolls until his curse is lifted.

Berevenenos hates dragons and dragonkind, and he is willing to give his weapons and armor to someone that impresses upon him their desire to fight against dragons without compromise. If an adventurer who takes Berevenenos’ weapons or armor later compromises with the dragons, Berevenenos will curse their clan until the weapons and armor are returned.

##### Characteristics
- INT 14
- POW 23 
- CHA 21
- Skills: Spirit Combat 115%.
- Languages: Speak Archaic Theyalan 80% (understood at ½ the listener’s best Theyalan language skill), Speak Spiritspeech 50%.

#### Grave Goods
The war panoply of Berevenenos is wrought of the finest craftsmanship of his age, as befits a king of his status and influence, with mighty and unique enchantments laid upon some of it. Fashioned and fitted for a human male of SIZ 18, the armor might be altered to fit another person of SIZ 16 to 19, at a cost of 10 L per piece and point of SIZ modified.
Possession and display of the helmet and cuirass will add +1 to the wearer’s CHA, as per the RuneQuest core rules on page 420.

Silver Closed Helmet: Crested with a horsehair ruff that has long deteriorated, the helmet provides 6 points of armor and contains a Demoralize spell matrix. It is ENC 2 and if sold, would be worth 350 L.

Iron Cuirass: This full torso cuirass covers the chest and abdomen and is coated in a chipped yet still-bright layer of enamel. It absorbs 9 pts. of damage and contains a Protection 4 spell matrix.
It is ENC 2, and if sold will bring the seller 900 L.

Great Shield: This huge shield resists each, all, and every attack by dragons and their kin so long as it is wielded actively or crouched behind.
It bears Berevenenos’ emblem upon the front.
Requires a STR of 24 for a single person to carry, the shield was normally carried by two warriors, and it is of a size to protect them both. It has a base SR 6, and if used as a weapon does 1D6 plus damage bonus. If two wielders are attacking or parrying with it, each must make a Large Shield roll: if either fails the whole action fails. Its ENC is 6 and selling it to the right buyer might be worth 750 L. Against draconic attacks it is invulnerable, and for other sources of harm it has 24 hit points.

Chest-breaker: This one-handed spear can pierce draconic skin or armor as if it did not exist. It otherwise is equivalent to a normal spear, though with double the normal hit points. It is worth at least 300 L to the right buyer.

Andrinor: This javelin can fly on its own accord, leaping from its wielder’s hands or even when set aside, so long as it is range. The chance of success is equal to the wielder’s normal Javelin skill. If it misses and is not stuck into the ground or into a solid surface (roll the wielder’s POW×5), it can be re-directed the next round. When first handled, this javelin requires a successful POW 15 resistance roll to master, but other than its magical properties, is otherwise identical to a normal javelin with 16 hit points. A fair price paid for it might net the seller 1,000 L, or even more.

### Old Top
This steep hill is sacred to Orlanth. The mostly treeless summit is covered with rock formations where sacrifices take place.

### Old Tower
This four-story stone tower dates to the Second Age. Both the Colymar and Lismelder tribes claim this ancient structure. It is currently not occupied by either tribe, but instead a refuge for bandits, members of the Elder Races, or worse things.

### Pegasus Plateau
This is a flat-topped mountain, rimmed by steep red stone cliffs that are over 250 meters high and almost impossible to climb. Only flying creatures can easily reach its summit.
A small herd of hippogriffs lives here, sacred to the Pure Horse People, has recently alighted here after being absent for centuries. They are said to nest atop the plateau.

### Pig Hollow
This narrow valley is heavily wooded with oak and pine.
Boars are common here, but only the most skilled or foolish warriors hunt in this area because of Redeye, the monstrous semi-divine boar that resides here.

#### RedEye the Boar
King Penterest fought against High King Tarkalor, Ernalda grew angry and sent a monstrous semi-divine boar to ravage the region. When the Colymar made peace and offered renewed sacrifices to the goddess, the boar’s rampages diminished, but it remains in Pig Hollow and sometimes lays waste to the fields in the nearby valleys.
Redeye is the size of a mammoth, intelligent and extremely aggressive. His razor-sharp silver tusks are long as shortswords and far more dangerous. Redeye is sacred to the Maran Gor cult and to the Tusk Riders. The Tusk Riders in Defending Apple Lane (page 79) worship Redeye as an incarnation of the God-Pig Gouger.

### Quackford
This market settlement is where the royal road crosses The Stream over Sartar’s Bridge, a stone bridge built by Prince Sartar. At 400 people, the village is the largest settlement of the Arnoring clan and includes a small community of ducks led by Fethry Hookbill and Mularda Bronzewings.
The village has a weekly Issaries market and boats take goods from here all the way to Esrolia.

### Queen’s Tomb
This tomb-sanctuary in the Thunder Hills is the burial place of an ancient demi-god ruler. It is covered by a huge mound and has a long entrance passageway. Huge statues of gods and goddesses are seated above the entrance. The gods and goddesses are seated, but the heads of the statues were at some point removed and they are now scattered throughout the area. Local herders avoid the place, claiming it is haunted.

The Queen’s Tomb is the resting place of Orgorvale Summer, the demi-goddess daughter of King Vingkot and granddaughter of Orlanth. She was the God Time ruler of these lands. Her cult died out in the Second Age, after the dream dragon Yerezum Storn was summoned here by draconic magic. Yerezum Storn has recently reappeared and resides within the Queen’s Tomb. See __The Dragon of the Thunder Hills__ (page 104).

### Quivin Mountains

This small mountain range consists of thirteen steep-sided peaks above 2,000 meters and incised by deep valleys. The highest peak is Quivin Mountain at 3,120 meters, and sacred to the Orlanth cult. Nearer to the Colymar lands are Wild Mountain (2,210 meters) and Eagles Mountain (2,560 meters). Above 2,500 meters or so there is usually snow and ice year-round. The tree line is around 1,750 meters.
The city of Boldhome is in a deep valley in the Quivin Mountains. The largest city in Sartar (and one of the largest in Dragon Pass), Boldhome is the traditional seat of the Princes of Sartar.

### Rainbow Mounds

Beneath these hills is a notorious system of caves, with walls colored in all the hues of the rainbow. The entrance is easily located because of an enormous plinth called the Giant’s Table, beneath which lies the entrance to the limestone caves.
The Giant’s Table is a double “T” formation, and the cave mouth lies between its legs. The plinth legs are 10 meters high and the stone topping them is about 12 meters long.

The caves beneath the Rainbow Mounds are home to a colony of newtlings and many rock lizards. From time to time, others have made their homes in the extensive caves; about a decade ago, a feared troll bandit gang used the caves as a hideaway.

### Runegate
The small walled town of Runegate squats atop a low rocky rise on the northern bank of the Swan River. It dates from before the Dragonkill War. Runegate was resettled by horse-worshipers who fled from the Holy Country in 1325, dragging their sacred lintel stone. They called themselves “Hyalorings” and claimed descent from the ancient hero Hyalor Horsebreaker. The Hyalorings formed a triaty of clans (the Enhyli, the Lonisi, and the Narri) with Runegate as their tribal center, and each clan swore to take wives only from a specific clan. However, around 1400, to make peace with the Colymar, the Narri took wives from them. This so-called “Fish Marriage” badly weakened the triaty so that the Lonisi were devastated in the subsequent Taral War. The remnants of the triaty eventually joined the Colymar tribe and the Taraling clan was founded on the ruins of the Lonisi.
The Taraling clan currently dominates the town.

Runegate was the site of a major battle when the Red Emperor invaded Sartar in 1602. The town survived an assault of undead from the Upland Marsh only to be destroyed by the Crimson Bat, only the lintel stone surviving.
It was subsequently rebuilt; as a result, most of the buildings are less than a generation old and most are built out of wood and lime plastered adobe. The town has a population of about 900 people, with a major temple to Elmal the Sun God and shrines to Humakt the War God and the Storm Bull. Queen Leika asserts lordship of Runegate; however, the chief of the Runegate town council, Verinos the Old, is generally seen as the master of the town.

To the north of Runegate is Horse Town, where the local clans breed, raise, and sell horses.

### Sacred Lands
These rich vineyards and fields belong to the Earth Temple.

### Salinarg’s Cairn

Part of the broken and desecrated body of Prince Salinarg was burnt here in 1602 and this cairn raised atop his remains.

It is sacred to Orlanth Rex and is regularly offered food and sacrifices.

Sartar’s Seal Bearer regularly appears near Salinarg’s Cairn.

### Sartar’s Seal Bearer
This ghostly entity typical manifests near Salinarg’s Cairn.
He appears as an elderly Sartarite nobleman, badly wounded and running from some unseen enemies. He calls out to the adventurers, asking them to aid him: “I failed my Prince. I swore to give Sartar’s ring to his heir.
My Prince is dead, buried beneath these stones; his heir is dead, slain by the Emperor. Help me fulfill my oath. Bring my Prince’s heir to his grave so I might give him Sartar’s ring.”

The man then displays Sartar’s Seal Ring—a large golden signet ring displaying a man being acclaimed by a goddess and a dragon. Any adventurer making a successful Homeland Lore (Sartar) roll recognizes the ring. The Seal Bearer pleads with the adventurers to help fulfill his oath.
If any adventurer refuses, he curses them (the effect being that the next time that adventurer tries to invoke their Loyalty (Sartar) Passion, the result is automatically treated as a fumble). If they accept his request, they must fulfil the request to the best of their abilities.

##### Characteristics
- INT 16
- POW 20 
- CHA 13
- Skills: Spirit Combat 88%.
- Languages: Speak Archaic Theyalan 80% (understood at ½ the listener’s best Theyalan language skill), Speak Spiritspeech 40%.

### Seven Falls
The Stream narrows here as it flows through a series of tiered cascades. None of the steps are much more than 4 meters, and salmon make the run up the cascades to Kjartan’s Lake every Earth Season.

### Stael’s Hills
This range of hills overlooks The Creek and separates the Colymar from the Malani tribe. The Boulder Ruins atop the hills form the traditional boundary between the Colymar and Malani tribes.

### Starfire Ridges
This rugged range of hills separates the Colymar from the Lismelder tribe. It is divided into the Little, Big, and Upper Starfire Ridges. It is a badland littered with rocks, ruins, caves, and strange obsidian formations; herders, hunters, and bandits are the main inhabitants.

### Stream Vale

This valley is densely settled with farms. The hills on the north side of the valley have many vineyards. Ducks populate the lower part of the valley. Centuries ago, the Colymar tried to seize control of the entire valley down to the Upland Marsh in the Beast Men Wars. Although the Colymar were initially successful, removing the ducks resulted in the Zombie War, which ended only once the Colymar recognized the duck’s claims to the lower part of the valley.

### Tarkalor Keep
This stone complex in Stael’s Hills consists of a tall central tower of some 25 meters high with four floors, and three additional towers of two floors. From atop the central tower, a watch can be kept on both the Ormthane and Swan valleys.
The tower was built to keep peace between the Colymar and Malani tribes, and the princes of Sartar often used the keep as a hunting lodge. The keep was abandoned during the Lunar Occupation and has served as a refuge for bandits, outlaws, and rebels.

### Tarndisi’s Grove
This ancient mixed forest of oak, beech, pine, and firs is sacred to the goddess Aldrya and inhabited by a small community of Aldryami, brown and green elves. Hunting and logging are strictly forbidden within the boundaries of the grove, although they are permitted in the surrounding woods. A small stand of redwood trees forms the dryad’s actual grove.

Tarndisi the Dryad, the Chief Priestess of Aldrya, rules the grove. She has friendly relations with the Clearwine Earth Temple and accepts offerings from the Ernalda cult. The Shanassee Tree is an ancient sentient redwood tree that dates to the Dawn.

#### Tarndisi the Dryad
Chief Priestess of Aldrya.

Female willow dryad, age 900+.

##### Characteristics
- STR 10
- CON 10
- SIZ 9
- INT 21
- DEX 17
- POW 33
- CHA 24
- Hit Points: 14
- Move: 9
- DEX SR: 1

- Runes: Earth 100%, Fertility 100%, Plant 100%.
- Rune Points: 31 (Aldrya)
- Rune Spells: Absorption, Accelerate Growth, Animate War Tree, Chameleon, Create War Tree, Dismiss Earth Elemental (small, medium), Gnome to Gargoyle, Heal Body, Plant Spy, Reflection, Resurrection, Silence Sphere, Summon Earth Elemental (small, medium).
- Spirit Magic: Befuddle (2 pt.), Detect Life, Extinguish, Food Song, Heal 6, Light, Shimmer 6, Vigor (2 pt.).
- Magic Powers: Second Sight ability (see page 264 of the RuneQuest core rules)—no magic point expenditure required. Additionally, she can leave her grove for up to one week once a year. She can form (or dissipate) her body from the vegetation of her grove at will. She may initiate spirit combat when discorporate, and for 1 magic point, she can command any incomplete creature born in her grove. The creature cannot resist this charm, which lasts for an hour.
- Magic Points: 63 (with allied spirit)
- Spirit Combat Damage: 3D6+4
- Passions: Hate (Darkness) 90%, Love (Shanassee Tree) 90%, Love (inhabitants of grove) 90%.

- Languages: Speak Aldryami 90%, Speak Auld Wyrmish 50%, Speak Beastspeech 60%, Speak Spiritspeech 60%, Speak Heortling 60%, Speak Tradetalk 60%, Read/Write Auld Wyrmish 50%.

#### Shanassee Tree
The Shanassee Tree serves as Tarndisi’s allied spirit. It is a subject of worship in its own right and is the mate/allied spirit of Tarndisi. It takes the form of an ancient redwood tree some 80 meters tall.

--|--|--|--|--
|Branch|55|1D6+3D6|6|As per branch

The War Tree can only attack with one branch each melee round. It can also parry with its branches.

### Elf Patrol
This is a typical group of green elves that might be encountered patrolling the woods around Tarndisi’s Grove.
They make sure that no enemies approach the Grove undetected. They do not disturb members of neutral or friendly cults (Lightbringers, Ernalda, Yelmalio, etc.), but they attack known enemies (Chaos, dwarves, trolls, or those that hunt or cut timber in the Grove).

The elves attack using their bows and are reluctant to engage in close combat.

### Thorn Top
Tanglethorns grow atop this hill at the edge of Tarndisi’s Grove.

### Thunder Hills
A mixture of oak groves and open grassland, the hills are sacred to Orlanth who often sends down strikes of lightning upon them.

### Two Faces
This steep stone hill has two heads carved on opposite sides of a U-shaped cliff; the heads appear to stare at each other.
There is an opening into the cliff past the heads, widely rumored to contain a great treasure.

### Two Sisters
Two small villages sit on either side of The Creek here. If the water is low, it is a ford; if the water is high, it is a ferry instead.

### Venharl’s Mound
This burial mound outside of Runegate is the grave of King Venharl and his companions who died in the Zarran War. It consists of a round burial chamber with a narrow corridor leading to the entrance. Within lies the burial urn of Venharl, his grave goods, and those of his companions who died with their king, protected by their ghosts.

#### Grave Goods
Iron cuirass (9 pts.), iron greaves and vambraces (9 pts.), iron closed helmet (8 pts.), iron broadsword (18 pts.) with Bladesharp 6 matrix, iron dagger, a quiver with 20 iron arrowheads (arrows warped with age), gold torc worth 1,500 L, gold armband worth 750 L, a silver fibulae worth 100 L, a gold ring with a carved seal worth 100 L, and decorated bronze and ceramic pottery for wine and feasting worth a grand total of roughly 500 L if it can be transported out safely and intact, cleaned, and a buyer found willing to purchase goods stolen from a grave.

## Neighbors
### Balmyr

The Balmyr tribe is a confederation of eight clans numbering about 7,600 people. They settled the southern reaches of Kjartan’s Lake and the broad valley to the south called Chormsland. The Balmyr are a member of the Wilmskirk confederation of tribes.

### Lismelder
The Lismelder are a small tribe located between the Upland Marsh and the Starfire Ridges. They number about 4,000 people (down almost 1,000 from the Great Winter) and consist of eight small clans. They were originally part of the Malani tribe but broke away in 1356. The Lismelder are noted friends of the ducks of Beast Valley and are their allies against the undead hosts of Delecti the Necromancer.

- Bostrop: This clan is famed for their dwarf-made water mill, which grinds grain into flour. Other communities bring their grain to them to be ground, making the Bostrop the richest clan of the Lismelder.
- Greydog: This small clan is best known for the Greydog Inn. They have long fought a losing battle with the Orlmarth clan over pasture rights on the Starfire Ridges.
- Goodsword: This clan controls the Six Stones, a minor temple to Humakt, also known as Indrodar’s Necklace.
- Goodweaver: At 800 people, this is the largest clan in the tribe. They are closely tied to the nearby duck communities and are known as peacemakers.
- Hillhaven: The smallest clan of the tribe, the Hillhaven number little more than 300 people. They have a long tradition of powerful Kolatings and has made many pacts with powerful local spirits.
- Lonendi: This clan is located near the Upland Marsh and is often troubled by the forces of Delecti the Necromancer.
- Marshedge: This clan is located on the edge of the Upland Marsh and has close links with the local ducks.
- Poss: This clan has an ancient rivalry with the Greydog clan. They prospered during the Lunar Occupation thanks to their co-operation with the Lunar Army.

### Locaem
This confederation of seven clans numbers about 7,000 people, and includes all the lands surrounding the Pegasus Plateau. The tribe is centered on the hill fort of Famegrave and is a member of the Wilmskirk confederation of tribes.
After the failure of Starbrow’s Rebellion, the Lunar Empire transferred the Zethnoring clan from the Colymar tribe to the Locaem tribe. The pro-Lunar king of the Locaem was devoured in the Dragonrise, and the tribe has been unable to agree upon a new king.

The Three-Emerald Temple is within the Locaem tribal lands. This major Earth Temple goes deep into the earth, where worshipers may offer sacrifices directly to the Three Green Goddesses.

### Malani
The Malani tribe is a confederation of six clans numbering about 6,000 people and is centered on Two Ridge Fort.
The Malani are a member of the Jonstown confederation of tribes. They have been ruled by a dynasty of Humaktworshiping kings; the current king is Amalda, the daughter of the previous king, Edruf Strongbreath.

The six clans are:

- Blue Fox Clan: This small clan occupies the area between Stael’s Hills and The Creek. The Blue Fox have friendly relations with the Narri clan.
- Desuring Clan: This small clan farms between Larnste’s Table and The Creek.
- Isolting Clan: The largest clan of the tribe, the Isolting is traditionally (although not always) the royal clan. They reside between Two Ridge Fort and The Creek. They are noted for their worship of Humakt the War God.
- Lysang Clan: One of the original Tree Triaty clans, the Lysang clan settled in the Arfritha Vale in the “Second Wave” of settlement. Their land-claims were disputed by the Colymar tribe, which was the cause of raids for many years. After the Zarran War, they acknowledged the Colymar tribe’s authority over the entire Arfritha Vale. In 1613, Lunar imperial authorities ordered the Lysang clan severed from the Colymar tribe and joined them to the Malani tribe.
- Namolding Clan: One of the original Tree Triaty clans, the Namolding clan settled in the Arfritha Vale in the “Second Wave” of settlement. They supported the Lysang clan in their dispute with the Colymar tribe. After the Zarran War, the Colymar were recognized as the rulers of the entire Arfritha Vale. The upper Arfritha Vale were given to the Antorling, who joined the Colymar tribe. The Namoldings gladly joined the Colymar tribe soon after. In 1613, Lunar imperial authorities ordered the Namolding clan severed from the Colymar tribe and joined them to the Malani tribe.
- Orleving Clan: This influential clan occupies the Ormsthane Vale. Ernalda is their patron deity and the major temple to the Earth Goddess at Greenstone is within their lands. They have feuded with the Varmandi clan for centuries.

### Sun Dome Temple
The Sun Dome Temple typically holds itself aloof from the Orlanthi-worshiping tribes of Sartar. The cults sometimes challenge each other ritually to commemorate the rivalry of Air and Sun and hold each other with distrust. The Runegate clans consider the cultists of the Sun Dome Temple to be traitors, despite (or perhaps because of ) the similarity of their cults.

### Upland Marsh
The Upland Marsh is a large valley bog, with a collection of murky islands, rocky outcroppings, and groves of cypress and blackthorn trees standing out of the dead plant material and black water. Several of the islands are the ruins of ancient cities and buildings. A maze of drainage channels crisscross the marsh, like a fine network of scars. Dead corpses animated by the evil magic of Delecti the Necromancer haunt the bog; some are little more than skeletons, the black bog water has preserved others with hair, organs, and skin intact despite the passage of centuries.

In 1380 the Lismelder and Colymar tribes attempted to seize the rich farmlands along the under-populated stretch of The Stream which is called Duck Vale. The only occupants were various Beast Men, especially the humanoid ducks. The attack was successful.

However, three years later came an invasion from the nearby Upland Marsh of walking corpses. Some were very old.
The Lismelder tribe called together its warriors and militia, but they were tricked and defeated in battle. The undead began burning all their houses and fields. An expedition led by a famous warrior went into the marsh. He was Kurash Varn, of the Sambari tribe. But he was ambushed by ducks, and the undead things just kept coming out. Divination revealed that the ducks were necessary to suppress the undead from the marsh.

At last a treaty was made, and the Colymar abandoned all the parts of Duck Vale which they had gone to, and paid compensation to the families of the dead.

# Apple Lane
The hamlet of Apple Lane is a small market settlement with approximately 20 adult inhabitants. It sits astride a well-used trade road on the borderlands between two tribes, some 32 kilometers from the nearest city, Jonstown. Travelers, from whom the settlement gets the larger share of its trade, are happy to stop at its inn and pray at the Uleria Temple. Farmers and shepherds from the surrounding hills enjoy its urbane comforts. Apple orchards, from which the hamlet draws its name, surround it on every side.

Apple Lane is near the boundary between the Colymar and Malani tribes. These tribes have old rivalries and disagreements that often result in raids and boundary disputes; occasionally in outright war. Both tribes follow Orlanth the Storm God. Most of the local population are members of the Hiording or Varmandi clans of the Colymar.

Apple Lane is part of the Kingdom of Sartar. Boldhome, capital city of Sartar, lies some 65 kilometers away by secondary and primary roads.

The map on the facing page shows the village buildings.
The descriptions of the individual buildings below contain notes about important people living there, and their statistics.
There are no stats for children: this is intentional and reflects the general practice among most Sartarites whereby the strong protect the weak.

Unless otherwise noted, all buildings are of stone and have terracotta roof tiles.

## Short Background
To help unite the tribes of the Quivin hills, Prince Sartar had a temple to Uleria built here. Several generations later, a companion of Prince Tarkalor, Gringle Goodsale, received the rights to establish a nearby market. A caravanserai called the Tin Inn was built alongside Gringle’s trade house, popularly called “Gringle’s Pawnshop,” and the hamlet took shape. Masons from Boldhome built many of the buildings.
The Colymar appointed a thane to protect the hamlet.
Travelers could pass through these lands unmolested and Apple Lane became a popular way station for traders.

The Lunar Occupation did not initially have much impact on Apple Lane and for a decade life continued as it did under the Princes of Sartar. After Starbrow’s Rebellion, Gringle found himself deeply in debt to the victorious Fazzur Wideread and received the dubious honor of the right to collect Lunar taxes from the Colymar and Malani tribes. In 1621, Lunars seeking Gringle’s treasures killed the Thane of Apple Lane, burned down the Pawnshop and gave the hamlet to a retired Lunar officer, Percippus.

The hamlet would have died but for the efforts of Brightflower the Brewer, Aileena the Priestess, and Hendroste Horsemaster, a new arrival. They kept the hamlet alive by encouraging travelers to come to Apple Lane for drinks, sex, and trade. They paid protection money to Percippus, who recruited several soldiers to help defend the hamlet.

After the Dragonrise of 1625, Lunar rule collapsed.
Percippus and his household fled, and Leika became Queen of the Colymar. Now the hamlet is free, but without protection.

## The Thane’s House
This sturdy two-story house is built out of stone. A stone cubicle attached to the house in back sometimes serves to detain breakers of the peace, although more often it is used for storage. This had been the home of Dronlan Swordsharp and his family, but it has stood empty for the last five years.


## The Thane’s Barn
This is one story, made of stone with a small hayloft.

## Uleria’s Temple and the Ladies Quarters
Uleria is the Goddess of Love, and this shrine is dedicated to her. The Uleria Temple is the central building in the hamlet and predates the rest of the village. It is a two-story stone building, its rectangular terracotta roof held up by columns carved and painted as coupled caryatids.

The temple has a different ornately carved door on each side; one entry is restricted to use by the priestess and her ladies and is covered with warnings to stay away. The other entries are dedicated to one of the goddess’ aspects, so that the priestess will know what duty to perform for each person who enters. One entry opens on a sacred bordello, where spells and rites are performed; one may give 5 L and couple with one of the initiates or 15 L and couple with the priestess.
A second door opens on a comfortable dining room where for 2 L each, strangers may converse, drink, and eat together.
The last door leads to the steam room, where for 1 L a guest may have a hot bath with water and steam. The internal walls have been whitewashed and painted with frescoes depicting a wide variety of erotic scenes. The second story holds the women’s living quarters.

One priestess and two younger ones live here, all tending the needs of their cult. The priestess Aileena is a strikingly attractive woman in her mid-30s. She has been with this temple for nearly 20 years and is devoted to the arts of Love. Janeera and Thrilla are younger women. Thrilla only recently came to the temple; she is a courtesan from the Lunar Empire that fled Boldhome after the Dragonrise and received Aileena’s protection. The temple performs fertility rituals, provides convivial company, and administers the arts of love as needed.

## New Skill
Courtesan (05): Courtesans are skilled professional craftspersons whose tools are the human body and it’s associated senses. This Communication skill represents the use of verbal enticement, creating an appropriate mood and tone for sexual encounters, seduction, bonding, pillow talk, and all activities appropriate to the role of the courtesan. The writeup of Uleria in Gods of Glorantha provides more information on this skill

### Aileena
Priestess of Uleria.

Female, age 34. Hiording clan.

Aileena is witty and charming, and possessed of great beauty.
She is devoted to the powers of Love and Life, and believes that her modest and remote temple has been chosen by the goddess to unite the war-torn and feuding peoples of Dragon Pass. To achieve the Eternal Moment of Allness, Aileena practices the Three Ways of Love: through the Body; through Community; and through Propagation. She casts her Rune magic for hire for anybody except creatures or worshipers of Chaos.

Aileena is well respected by the Colymar and Malani tribes.

## Weaponsmasters’ Guild Hall
This is another two-story stone building. The ground floor is larger than the upper floor. The building is currently vacant, although Squinch pays for it to be cleaned each season.

Downstairs has several empty rooms, and a shrine to the gods of war and battle (Humakt, Orlanth, and Babeester Gor). Upstairs are two sleeping rooms.

## The Tin Inn
The Tin Inn is a well-established and fortified roadhouse on a frequently travelled route, and almost always has visitors. It is the only tavern for a day’s journey beyond; outlying farmers normally congregate at the inn. The inn has two stories and it, along with its outbuildings, wall, and courtyard are tin-roofed. The inn has a large common room on the ground floor; upstairs are eight rooms (although only five are available to rent). Paying guests may sleep in the common room or rent out the upstairs rooms (which are equipped with a bed, a washing basin, and a chamber pot). Prices for drinks, food, and accommodations are provided on page 413 of the RuneQuest core rules.

Brightflower, the daughter of the previous owner, runs the inn. Gooseberry Sprig works as the cook and dogsbody.

Postal and Mineera live in a small building within the walls but outside the inn and work there as well. Postal is the stable hand as well as being noted for his skill at carpentry.
Mineera gardens and tends the animals. Their daughters Jeena and Vareena work in the inn as waitresses and general helpers. Jeena is married to Hendroste the Horsemaster and resides with him.

Pramble, an elderly poet, lives permanently in the Tin Inn, as he has for the last 20 years. He has a hunched posture and personality, and spends most of his meager income on vellum and ink. Occasionally he works as an extra for the inn when he needs money and when trade allows.

Squinch, the local scholar, also lives at the Tin Inn. See Storeroom (page 69) for more about him).

#### Brightflower
Initiate of Ernalda, lay member of Issaries.

Female, age 24. Varmandi clan.

Brightflower is the daughter of Bulster Brewer and his wife Bertha. Both died in the Great Winter. Her brother Barayo left Apple Lane 15 years ago to be a mercenary in the Holy Country, where he is rumored to be a mercenary captain.
With her father, mother, and husband all dead, Brightflower now manages the inn and serves as the brewer. She is famed for her fruit and barley wines.

Gooseberry Sprig is a self-serving thief and con man. He is also a talented and inexpensive cook, which is why Brightflower has put up with him so far. He’s been at the Tin Inn since the Great Winter and he is surprisingly loyal to Brightflower. Gooseberry is friends with a gourmand satyr named Toro and a music-loving centaur named Sonzog, who occasionally visit Apple Lane; they were once part of a local bandit gang.

## Temple To All The Deities

This is a two-story building with a full basement. An additional two-story tall tower surmounts it.

Of the main building, the first floor is one large room, while the upper floor is divided into several smaller rooms.
These rooms are decorated with frescoes showing as many deities as the painters could imagine. In the basement are rooms for storing food and equipment, and the caretaker’s rooms.

The first and second floors of the temple, as well as the two tower rooms, are rented for celebrations or sacrifices.
Locals use them for their seasonal farming ceremonies, and for weekly prayers to less popular deities. Only cults of Chaos and evil have ever been denied rental here.

No one has ever dared to try to rob the place, though rumor has it that several guardian spirits occupy the building.
Even during the depths of the Lunar Occupation, this building was unspoiled.

The caretaker is an old woman, Kareena. Kareena still mourns for her ward, a simple girl who died in the Great Winter.

## Horsemasters’ Guild Hall And Residence
One-half of this one-story building is a single large room.
There is a votive tablet of a horseman holding a lance—a common depiction of Hyalor the Horseman.

The other half of the building houses Hendroste the Horsemaster, his wife Jeena, and her four-year old son Jenestan. Hendroste is a Grazelander who came here two years ago with a string of fine horses, seeking adventure and following a mysterious vision that led him to Apple Lane.

## Stable
The Horsemasters’ stable has 80 stalls. In addition to training horses, Hendroste also breeds and sells them. He has 2–12 horses available, as well as a few mules and donkeys (never more than ten at a time).

Half of the stable has a second story, used for storage.

## Storeroom
This one-story building has a unique (and very secure) pyramid shaped roof made of stone. The walls and foundations are also of stone. A single brassbound and magically-locked door (locked with a Lock spell backed up with 10 magic points) faces the Tin Inn. The storeroom has never been robbed.

The storeroom is used as a regional granary. It also functions as the hamlet’s general store and is run by Squinch.
Within the granary are foodstuffs, grain, apples, wine, seeds, cloth, blank parchment, and just about any other common trade good that Squinch thinks he can sell to the villagers, travelers, or farmers.

Though he is aloof and artificially worldly, he never intentionally cheats a customer. Squinch’s air of snobbery comes from his position as the local sage and merchant. The farmers and most travelers go to him for their needs, and he also acts as the buyer of surplus farm crops.

Squinch lives in the Tin Inn, where usually he can be found.
