### Gringle’s Pawnshop
This is the burnt husk of a two-story stone building. The roof is gone (having fallen into the building), and one secondfloor wall has collapsed. This austere two-story stone building has only one window (on the second story), and only two doors. It is called Gringle’s Pawnshop after its original owner, a merchant priest who ran the pawnshop for nearly 50 years.

### Smithy and Armory
This building is empty and abandoned. The double doors of this building face the road. There also is a door in the rear.

### Ruined Hut
This is little more than a campsite, surrounded by a broken fence.

### Issaries Shrine
This is a stone that has been carved with a crude image of Issaries and his runes placed upon it. Travelers pay their respect to the God of Travelers by offering it a libation of wine or placing food at the base of the stone.

### Surrounding Area
Orchards, mainly the apples from which the village takes its name, surround the community for several kilometers, interspersed with farmhouses and pasturage. Tenants from the nearby Varmandi and Hiording clans work the orchards.
Migrants and fugitives sometimes camp in the trees.

The orchards surrounding Apple Lane produce the equivalent of 25 hides of food, primarily apples. The orchard rights are distributed as follows: five hides for the support of the Uleria Temple, five hides for the support of the Horsemasters’ Guild, and five for the support of the Temple to All the Deities. Another five hides are customarily allocated to the thane and five more hides to the Weaponsmasters’ Guild, but as that office is vacant, the produce is currently sold by Squinch, and given to the tribe.

# Rumors
These rumors are intended as spurs to adventure or roleplaying opportunities. When in an area where rumors could reasonably be encountered (e.g., towns and villages, but not wilderness), the adventurers can encounter one or more rumors chosen by the gamemaster or randomly generated from the following list. The gamemaster should present the rumors in an interesting and natural way, treating each as a guideline, rewording it based on the situation in which it is encountered.

To randomly determine a rumor, the gamemaster should roll a D100.
Each rumor is prefaced by a single letter indicating its veracity: T: The rumor is true, F: The rumor is false, M: The rumor is meaningless, R: The rumor may or may not be true at the gamemaster’s discretion, B: The rumor is generally true but also has a substantial false component.

## Rumors (1-16)

|Result|Type|Rumor
--|--|--
|1|B|Argrath Whitebull is raising an army of Praxian barbarians to conquer Sartar and make himself Prince.
|2|T|Kallyr Starbrow is preparing to perform the Lightbringers’ Quest of Harmast Barefoot!
|3|F|Jar-eel the Razoress is in Furthest and raising a new Lunar Army to reconquer Dragon Pass.
|4|F|Harrek the Berserk is marching north with an army of Wolf Pirates to plunder Dragon Pass.
|5|F|The new Feathered Horse Queen is an initiate of the Red Goddess!
|6|T|When the elves wear red and yellow in Storm Season the forest is planning for war.
|7|F|The Lunar Empire has collapsed and Pentan nomads can be seen outside the walls of Glamour.
|8|B|The trolls have successfully ended the Curse of Kin and are about to re-impose the Shadow Tribute on Dragon Pass.
|9|B|The Lunar Empire has contacted Delecti and has offered to let him turn the Sartarite dead into zombies.
|10|F|King Broyan has returned from the dead and has united the Holy Country.
|11|T|Queen Leika and Kallyr Starbrow are rivals over the leadership of Sartar.
|12|B|Argrath Whitebull is a Dragon Friend.
|13|T|The new Feathered Horse Queen seeks to become the High Queen of Dragon Pass.
|14|B|Fazzur Wideread has reconciled with King Pharandros to fight the Sartarites.
|15|F|Kallyr Starbrow is planning to replace Queen Leika with one of her followers.
|16|B|The Lismelder have allied with ducks and are seeking a new war against the Colymar.
|17|B|The Jonstown tribes have an alliance against the Colymar.
|18|T|The Telmori werewolves have sworn loyalty to Kallyr Starbrow.
|19|F|Kallyr Starbrow has relit the Flame of Sartar.
|20|T|A dream dragon has been seen ravaging farms in Sartar.
|21|F|Harrek the Berserk has sacked Nochet and killed Queen Samastina.
|22|R|Former Lunar supporters in the tribes are planning to assassinate Kallyr Starbrow.
|23|T|The Old Grave is the resting place of a great treasure but guarded by a powerful and terrible spirit.
|24|R|A foreign magician and a gang of bandits have taken over the Old Tower.
|25|R|Ogres are posing as tinkers and other travelers and eating people in secrecy.
|26|R|Kallyr Starbrow plans to reclaim Tarkalor Keep and garrison it with mercenaries from other tribes to keep a check on the Colymar.
|27|T|Dragonewts can be placated by placing raw meat and flowers at the little altars near their plinths.
|28|B|High Priestess Ereneva Chan is more loyal to the priestesses of Nochet than she is to the Colymar tribe.
|29|F|Kallyr Starbrow was behind the assassination of her predecessor, Prince Temertain.
|30|F|Queen Leika plans to lead a revolt against Kallyr Starbrow and make herself Prince of Sartar.
|31|T|Nameless has sworn to defend Queen Leika from assassins.
|32|T|The Lunar Empire still has many secret supporters in Sartar who work for a Lunar restoration.
|33|B|Kerisdana Hollybright was expelled from the Sisters of Mercy Temple near Battle Valley because of her adventures with Leika Ballista.
|34|F|Sora Goodseller is dangerously reckless and untrustworthy. She even joined the Etyries cult and then left the cult once Lunar rule collapsed.
|35|F|The best way to get justice at the tribal assembly is to bribe Hastur the Lawspeaker to support your case.
|36|T|Hastur the Lawspeaker will pay well for esoteric information or lore and he’s got a small library of information stored away in the royal palace.
|37|F|The elves hate everyone who has ever cut down a tree or plowed a field—anywhere, and not just in the elf forests or sacred groves.
|38|T|Argrath Whitebull is in league with Praxian barbarians, Wolf Pirates, foreign sorcerers, mad dragonspeakers, and even Tricksters. He is not to be trusted!
|39|B|The dryad of Tarndisi’s Grove once had a human husband, but she became a tree after he died.
|40|T|The ducks know the safe paths through the Upland Marsh.
|41|R|There is a secret cavern beneath the Boulder Ruins which contains a repository of secrets about the Empire of the Wyrms Friends.
|42|T|The Lunar Empire waxes and wanes like the Red Moon does; it may be waning now, but it will wax again.
|43|B|The Lunars broke into the Old Grave and found great treasures within.
|44|B|Delecti the Necromancer was once a functionary in the Empire of the Wyrms Friends who survived death by inhabiting cadavers.
|45|B|Triallinon’s Harp would cut through this noise. Too bad the satyrs at Ash Anvari won’t give it up under any circumstance.
|46|B|The Lost Grazer clan came back from the West but now they’re riding unicorns.
|47|R|Great Bearded Bran wants a black thunderstone from the Duran Hills.
|48|R|The dryad is inviting warriors to a party at the Stone Tree.
|49|B|Issaries is initiating everyone who can impress the examiners and count to eleven.
|50|F|Either ducks have no women or even they can’t tell the difference. No wonder they’re angry all the time!
|51|R|The assassin-hunting trickster known only as the Woodpecker has come home to retire.
|52|T|Forang Farosh, the reincarnated scholar who once lived during the Empire of the Wyrms Friends and now lives in the village of Tink, is willing to provide information about the Second Age for a price.
|53|T|A herd of winged horses live atop the Pegasus Plateau; if someone could climb up there and tame one, they’d be set for life!
|54|R|Wherever he’s wandered off to, Aristos the Philosopher usually sobers up and offers a boon to whoever is first on the scene with a meal. That should be about now.
|55|R|Crazy dragonewts! A priest and its entourage are hiring interpreters for “a journey to the West via Balazar.” Naturally, I recommended you.
|56|B|Argrath faked the appearance of the white bison and tricked the Praxians into following him. But don’t tell the Animal Nomads that or they’ll slit your throat!
|57|B|Dunorl the Old is more loyal to the House of Sartar than he is to his own clan and tribe.
|58|F|The Temple of the Wooden Sword disbanded because one of their members betrayed the temple to the Lunar Empire. Nameless is the only survivor of that treachery.
|59|T|Queen Leika was preparing to assassinate King Kangharl but the True Dragon beat her to it!
|60|T|A ghost haunts Salinarg’s Cairn.
|61|F|The Crimson Bat is on its way to Sartar, led by Jar-eel the Razoress and the Lunar College of Magic.
|62|T|The new chief of the Varmandi clan, Korol Serpent-Tongue, is a troublemaker. He humiliates the Malani by ignoring their boundary claims. The Malani will eventually strike back, but I suspect that is what Korol wants.
|63|R|The Konthasos clan worries about this winter’s clearwine harvest and opposes further wars now that the Colymar are free from the Lunar Empire.
|64|B|The Ernaldori clan and the Earth Priestesses have too much say over the Colymar tribe and secretly wish to have the High Priestess be the real ruler of the tribe like in Esrolia.
|65|R|The Greydog clan claim that their barleywines are healthier and tastier than even sacred clearwine and have bribed the ducks to dump shipments of Colymar wine going downriver.
|66|B|The Uleria priestesses of Apple Lane are philosophers more than courtesans. They know secrets from the Cosmic Court which they communicate through their ecstatic rites.
|67|R|A band of Beastmen and rogue dragonewts are ambushing travelers at Birne’s Squeeze.
|68|T|A demonic hound haunts the hills above Tarndisi’s Grove.
|69|R|A vampire from the Upland Marsh resides in the Odd Tower.
|70|R|Sora Goodseller and her White Grape Inn has been bribing farmers to buy most of the upcoming clearwine vintage and sell the wine in Nochet at five times its usual price.
|71|B|The ram’s head atop the entry to the Ram’s Head Inn has a magical ability to detect Lunars and other enemies of the Colymar. It secretly warns the inn’s owner and the locals make sure that the visitor is never seen from again.
|72|T|Queen Leika has little support amongst her own Taraling clan. She may call herself the Lord of Runegate, but the real ruler there is Verinos the Old, the head of the town council.
|73|F|The Enhyli clan leaders are negotiating with the Sun Dome Temple to have Elmal recognized as a subcult of Yelmalio.
|74|R|The women born to the Hiording clan are given a cloak of swan feathers at their initiation and can shapechange into a swan.
|75|R|The Anmangarn are accepting new members. All you must do is find the Black Spear somewhere inside Tarndisi’s Grove, and then follow the spear to a secret place. And not get killed by elves, monstrous boars, or malevolent spirits in the process!
|76|T|The sacred woodpeckers of the Orlmarthings have said that Sartar’s heir was born on the Starfire Ridges. The woodpeckers never lie.
|77|R|Kallyr Starbrow is outraged that the Antorling clan has imposed tolls to cross The Stream at Sartar’s Bridge. The Prince has demanded that Queen Leika remove the tolls.
|78|R|The Enjossi clan leaders are secretly advised by a giant talking salmon that drank deep from the Well of Nelat and now lives in Kjartan Lake.
|79|B|The Narri clan are using Goldeneye stallions stolen from the Grazelanders to stud and selling the hybrid offspring at the Horse Town market.
|80|T|Dragonewts have been using their magical roads to transport an invisible army that could destroy the Colymar overnight!
|81|T|The dream dragons that rampage across the countryside are but the figments of the imagination of a True Dragon. The True Dragon awakened with the Dragonrise is far greater threat to us than the Lunar Empire ever could be.
|82|B|The Balmyr tribe has allied with the Merchant Prince Gold-Gotti to get rich off trade with Kethaela.
|83|B|The Malani tribe has allied with Kallyr Starbrow to keep the Colymar clans and lands that the Lunars gave them.
|84|R|Without a tribal king to unite then, the Locaem clans are fighting among themselves. The tribe is either about to disintegrate.
|85|R|A clandestine temple to the Seven Mothers is hidden somewhere in Clearwine Fort, maintained by a handful of secretive cult members.
|86|F|Harrek the Berserk is marching on Dragon Pass with an army of Wolf Pirates and intends on enslaving or killing everyone.
|87|R|Ethilrist and his Black Horse Troop is searching for magical artifacts in the hills of Sartar.
|88|R|Dwarves have been seen wandering around the hills between the Colymar and Malani tribal lands, looking for ancient treasures.
|89|B|The Shaker Priestess has been offering human sacrifices to Maran Gor.
|90|R|The Malani tribe is planning a preemptive attack on the Varmandi clan and are recruiting mercenaries from the Humakt cult.
|91|R|The Lismelder tribe seeks vengeance against the Orlmarth clan because of their feud with the Grey Dogs. The Lismelder are recruiting mercenaries from the Humakt cult.
|92|R|A Storm Khan and his Praxian followers have been wandering around the hills, terrorizing everyone they meet. They claim to be hunting Chaos, but I think they are bandits.
|93|T|There is a troupe of inhuman entertainers that travels through Dragon Pass. The manager is a satyr, the drummer is a giant, and the other musicians are animated skeletons!
|94|T|There is a veteran mercenary named Raveena who claims to be the daughter of the deceased Thane of Apple Lane. She claims to have been away for many years fighting in Balazar and the Holy Country, and now wishes to reclaim her father’s office.
|95|B|Those Lhankor Mhy sages are no better than the God Learners. They pry out cult lore and other secrets and use it for their own purposes!
|96|F|King Pharandros has made peace with his uncle Fazzur Wideread and ended the Tarshite Civil War. Sartar is doomed!
|97|B|Delecti the Necromancer has sent female vampires called the Dancers in Darkness to expand his dominion.
|98|T|Tarndisi the Dryad has lived in her grove since the Dawn. She knows more about these lands and its spirits than any human.
|99|M|Never trust a duck.
|100|M|There is a shaman who says he has seen an infinite number of worlds, and each vary from this one to a greater or lesser degree. In one, the Lunar Empire still rules Dragon Pass; in another, the Red Moon never rose. In one, a terrible monster sleeps beneath the sea and will destroy the world when it awakens; in another, Belintar still reigns in the Holy Country.

# Defending Apple Lane
This scenario is intended for four to six starting adventurers. At least one should be a member of the Colymar tribe and at least one should be an initiate of Orlanth or an associated cult. Some, although not necessarily all, of the adventurers should be reasonably skilled at combat (75% or better), as the scenario requires that the adventurers defend the hamlet of Apple Lane from a band of Tusk Riders.

## Gamemaster Background
With the collapse of Lunar rule in Dragon Pass resulting from the Dragonrise, many have taken to banditry. Few bandits are more feared than the bands of Tusk Riders who pillage and murder from atop their giant boars.

Xiobalg’s band is one of these—former mercenaries for the Lunar Empire who now prey on whoever they want. A few days ago, Xiobalg raided several farms near Apple Lane, but was angered that there was so little to plunder. After Yaram tortured a captured farmer named Saraskos, Xiobalg learned the reason: the harvest was stored in the Apple Lane granary, along with other goods. Xiobalg told the apparently dying Saraskos that he planned to raid Apple Lane soon and would pillage the hamlet—and its Uleria temple—and raze it to the ground.

Despite all odds, Saraskos survived and made his way to Apple Lane to warn Aileena and Brightflower of the Tusk Riders’ plans. The women recognized that the hamlet could not defend itself—they would need to hire defenders.
Fortuitously, the adventurers arrive in Apple Lane soon after.

### Current Events
This scenario is assumed to take place in Earth Season 1625, a week or two after the harvest. Only a few weeks before was the cataclysmic event known as Dragonrise. The heavens shook: the constellation known as Orlanth’s Ring appeared on the wrong day and quickly spiraled around the sky as several planets and constellations streaked out of place to explode against the orange stars. Other stars disappeared or fell out of the sky. Finally, an impossibly huge True Dragon erupted out of the ground some 60 kilometers to southeast and then rose into the sky. In a matter of minutes, the bulk of the Lunar Army in Dragon Pass was gone. For a week, the True Dragon could be seen in the sky, blocking out the Red Moon.

Sartar has been thrown into turmoil with the collapse of Lunar rule. Queen Leika returned to Clearwine Fort with her supporters and was hastily acclaimed tribal king. The other tribes of Sartar have also proclaimed their independence from the Lunar Empire. The sacred city of Boldhome has already fallen to the warlord Kallyr Starbrow and she has acclaimed herself Prince of Sartar, but there remains a small Lunar garrison in Jonstown.

The timeless ritual of the harvest carried on despite these world-shaking events. Cereals, apples, grapes, and figs all need to be harvested, and so despite the fears of war and dragons, life has carried on.

## Involving the Adventurers
The adventurers need only be passing through Apple Lane to get involved in this adventure; perhaps they are returning from cult activities, from visiting kinfolk, traveling along the trade roads, or even going to visit the Uleria Temple. Apple Lane is a local crossroads and travelers are common there.

### Meeting Aileena and Brightflower
The Tin Inn is quiet and largely empty—bandits have troubled this area and travelers are scarce in this time of war and strife. Only the inn staff, Aileena the Priestess, and Hendroste Horsemaster are present. Brightflower the innkeeper provides the adventurers with a round of drinks on the house—while the adventurers are drinking, Aileena the Priestess, Brightflower, and Hendroste Horsemaster join them.

Aileena introduces herself as the local Priestess of Uleria.
She is an attractive woman in her mid-30s, wearing kohl and fragrant perfume. She asks about the adventurers’ deeds, comments on their weapons, any markings of war gods (such as Orlanth, Argan Argar, Babeester Gor, Humakt, Maran Gor, Storm Bull, Waha, Yelmalio, or Yelm), notable scars, and so on—she seeks to ascertain that the adventurers include warriors (or at least competent fighters). Once she is satisfied that the adventurers might be competent fighters, she gets right to the point:

“We have desperate need of your prowess! Apple Lane is in terrible danger—a gang of Tusk Rider bandits is threatening to destroy our community. We are a small community and, other than Hendroste here, we have no warriors. We wish to hire you to defend Apple Lane from these Tusk Riders.” The adventurers likely have questions:

#### Q: How do you know this?
A: “A local farmer, named Saraskos, was captured and tortured by the Tusk Riders. While being tortured, he overheard that the Tusk Rider chief planned to come to Apple Lane and pillage the hamlet and the storeroom. Despite his wounds, Saraskos managed to escape and made it here with the news.
He is recovering from his wounds in the Temple.”

#### Q: What do you know about Tusk Riders?
A: “Tusk Riders are hate-filled hybrids of men and trolls.
They ride giant boars and offer human sacrifice to their chthonic gods. They enjoy torturing and killing their victims.”

#### Q: Why can’t Uleria protect you?
A: “My goddess is not a martial deity—she is the power of Love, not Death. With time, I have no doubts She could demonstrate that embracing Love is preferable to embracing Death, but I do not believe these Tusk Riders will give us that opportunity. I fear they must be confronted with force.
Such is the broken world we mortals inhabit.”

#### Q: How many Tusk Riders are there?
A: “Saraskos mentioned that there were less than a dozen.”

#### Q: How many fighters are here in Apple Lane?
A: “Other than Hendroste here, I think only Postal even knows how to handle a spear. And old Kareena the Godtalker, of course.”

Brightflower handles negotiations for remunerations. She offers 10 L for each adventurer to defend the village, plus free food, drink, and lodging in the Tin Inn until the Tusk Riders are driven off. Brightflower offers 10 Wheels for the leader of the Tusk Riders—dead or alive, plus another 1 Wheel for each additional Tusk Rider killed or captured. She can be bargained up as per the RuneQuest rules (page 168).

If the adventurers seem reluctant, Aileena will appeal to their Honor or test their Fertility Rune. She is desperate for their aid and will offer whatever she can, including the blessings of her goddess.

## The Villagers
Aileena, Brightflower, and Hendroste have the villagers gather at the Tin Inn so that the adventurers can discuss their plan of defending the village. There is a total of thirteen adults in the hamlet. As Aileena warned, few of the residents have any proficiency at fighting. Although Hendroste Horsemaster and Kareena the God-talker are skilled fighters, and Postal served in the local militia in his youth, the other villagers are effectively noncombatants. They either cannot or will not fight against Tusk Riders except in the direst of circumstances.

If the gamemaster believes that the adventurers have a fair or better chance of defeating the Tusk Riders on their own, then Postal plans to defend the Tin Inn, Hendroste plans to defend his horses, and Kareena plans to defend the Temple to All the Deities and heal the wounded. It will require successful Orate or Fast Talk skill tests to persuade them to alter their plans.

If the gamemaster believes that the adventurers are grossly outnumbered or outclassed by the Tusk Riders, then Postal, Hendroste, and even Kareena can directly assist the adventurers (depending on how outclassed the adventurers are).

The villagers are most insistent that their lives are preserved and that the Uleria temple, Temple to All the Deities, Tin Inn, and the Storeroom are defended. Other buildings are acknowledged as expendable.

Let the players describe their plan for defending Apple Lane. Any plan should answer the following questions at the very least:
- How do they watch for the Tusk Riders?
- Where are the combatants located? Are they on foot or mounted? Are they stationary or are they patrolling?
- Where are the non-combatants?

If the players’ plan does not include this information, the gamemaster may ask suitable characters to make Battle rolls to realize that these critical elements are missing from the plan. If this is unsuccessful, the gamemaster may choose to exploit this lack of preparedness as seems appropriate.

## The Attack
### The Feint
The initial attack on Apple Lane is a feint. Mardu and Yodram ride in from the North Street towards the Uleria Temple, whooping and yelling. They plan to ride past the Uleria Temple towards the orchards to the west of the hamlet, trying to draw off any defenders. Meanwhile, the rest of the Tusk Riders hide in the orchards south of the old Smithy. If either takes enough damage to incapacitate a hit location, they ride away, blowing their horns in panic.

### Rush for the Gate
Once Mardu and Yodram ride past the Well (or if Mardu or Yodram blow their horn in panic), the rest of the Tusk Riders ride out. Zeoldan, Durug, Xiobalg, and Qualbalg form a single file line and charge the gate of the Tin Inn with the Tuskers. It will take 3 full melee rounds for them to arrive at the gate of the Tin Inn—each Tusker will slam into the gate, starting at SR 3, then (assuming the first boar fails to crash the gate) SR 6, then SR 9, then SR 12. Each boar does damage equivalent to its Gore attack to the gate.
The gate has 18 hit points and 10 armor points: attacks that do damage for less than the armor points will tear into the wood but are otherwise ineffectual.

If all four boars fail to break the gate, they will withdraw for a round, turn around and charge again (taking two complete rounds). They will repeat this as long as is necessary or feasible.

### Roving Reinforcements
Kranag and Yaram act as roving reinforcements. If Mardu and Yodram are under attack, they come to reinforce them.
If Mardu and Yodram have drawn off defenders, Kranag and Yaram attack the defenders from the rear.

If Xiobalg’s group gets unexpected resistance at the Tin Inn, Kranag and Yaram ride to reinforce them.

### Breaking Into the Tin Inn
If the Tusk Riders break through the gate of the Tin Inn, they will enter the courtyard the following round. They search within looking for hostages from among the non-combatants.
If there are none in the courtyard, they enter the Inn itself.

With hostages in hand, Xiobalg proposes a deal—he offers to return the hostages in exchange for the defenders leaving the village to the Tusk Riders to be pillaged. Xiobalg plans to renege on the deal if possible—the moment the defenders leave their defensive positions, he will order an attack on the defenders.

### Chasing off the Tusk Riders
If any of the Tusk Riders are killed or incapacitated, all Tusk Riders except Xiobalg and Kranag need to roll for an appropriate Passion to stay engaged in melee. If a Tusk Rider fails, they will disengage and try to retreat. If at least half of the surviving Tusk Riders flee, Xiobalg and Kranag will also try to withdraw—they do not need to make a Passion roll.

Retreating Tusk Riders will retreat to the safety of Pig Hollow in the Thunder Hills. They are easy to track (double the effective Track skill of the trackers). Once at Pig Hollow, they offer blood and promises to Redeye the Boar if it will protect them.

## Pig Hollow
Pig Hollow is a narrow “V”-shaped valley in the Thunder Hills. It was cut by a seasonal stream and runs less than five kilometers. Hound Knob rises some 600 meters above the bottom of the hollow, while the Dragonewt Plinth on the eastern hills is some 400 meters above the floor. The floor of the valley is heavily wooded with oak and pine, and rich with animal life. There are numerous spirits in the valley, but none will engage in Spirit Combat with corporeal beings.

A successful Track can locate the camp. If the adventurers are following the giant Tusker boars, it is easy to track them to their lair (see Chasing Off the Tusk Riders, page 78), otherwise they need to make a normal Track roll.

If there are no Tusk Riders at their lair (for example if all the attackers were killed or taken captive), then there is 20% chance that Redeye the Boar will be near the Tusk Rider lair.
If Tusk Riders are present, there is a 50% chance that Redeye the Boar will be nearby.

Give the adventurers the opportunity to flee from Redeye the Boar, as he can easily wipe out a party of unprepared adventurers! Redeye does not pursue fleeing adventurers.

The Tusk Rider camp consists of pine branches and other vegetation piled up high enough to serve as shelters for the Tuskers. There is a clearing around the fire pit and a midden pile of bones. A successful Search roll locates the Tusk Riders treasure hoard, buried in the midden pile: 617 L, 15 golden Wheels, a golden arm ring (worth 350 L), a golden neck ring (worth 1,250 L), three golden pendants (worth 950 L each), a golden fibula (worth 300 L), and six silver dishes (two silver skillets, one worth 250 L and the other decorated with the figure of Asrelia and worth 325 L; a large fluted washingbowl with Esrola, Ernalda, and Maran Gor in the center and worth 300 L; another fluted dish worth 400 L; and a large silver plate and small silver cup worth 250 L and 200 L).

### The Tusk Riders
Xiobalg’s band of eight Tusk Riders has taken advantage of the collapse of Lunar rule in Sartar to plunder and pillage to their black hearts’ content. For the last season, they have used a camp near Pig Hollow as a refuge. From there, this small band of marauders has, with impunity, terrorized the locals and any traveling without a sizable escort.

This cocky group scoffs at the suggestion that the local farmers pose any threat. The arrogant Xiobalg leads the raiders. More feared than admired, Xiobalg’s words are closely heeded. The grizzled veteran, Kranag, is Xiobalg’s second-in-command.

#### Xiobalg
Initiate of the Bloody Tusk.

Male Tusk Rider, age 30+.

Xiobalg is ready to claim Rune Lord status upon his return to the Ivory Plinth. He plans to sacrifice any hostages there and bind their spirits into their hands. Xiobalg is cruel but clever. His strategies tend to be simple and effective. He possesses a POW-storing crystal containing 11 magic points and a necklace with two severed hands (both human), each containing the bound spirit of its prior owner. Xiobalg can order the spirits to cast spells they possess.

Before engaging in combat, Xiobalg will spend two rounds to have his spirits cast Countermagic, Bladesharp, and Protection. He will then cast Demoralize on the most dangerous looking foe and charge.

If pressed, Xiobalg will use his remaining Rune points to cast Pain Tooth (see page 87), but not before, He has an ivory wand, valued at 500 L, which has a Befuddle matrix.
He also owns a necklace worth 300 L, with an Extinguish matrix on it.

## Consequences
If the adventurers defeat or chase off the Tusk Riders, they each get a +1D3% increase in their Reputation. They also get whatever reward they negotiated with Aileena and Brightflower.

### Thane of Apple Lane
If the adventurers were successful, acted honorably towards the villagers, were suitably respectful of Aileena and her initiates, and at least one is an initiate of Orlanth or of an associated cult, Aileena offers them something greater than mere coin: the title of Thane of Apple Lane and the buildings and land that go with it. The prospective thane must:

- Be an initiate of Orlanth (or an associated cult).
- Swear to defend Apple Lane, uphold the law of the tribe and of Sartar.
- Swear to serve the Colymar tribe in war and peace.

The oath is performed in the Temple to All the Deities before a votive image of Orlanth, with all the villagers (and many nearby farmers) present.

Afterwards, the newly acclaimed Thane of Apple Lane must travel to Clearwine Fort (about 30 kilometers away) to be recognized by Queen Leika. The new thane must take Loyalty (Colymar Tribe) or Loyalty (Queen Leika) at 60% or add +20% to one of these existing Passions. In return, the queen presents the new thane with an exceptionally fine broadsword (1D8+2 damage, 13 HP, worth 150 L) and confirms their status. The ceremony serves to permanently increase the new thane’s CHA by +1.

### Benefits of Being the Thane of Apple Lane

The Thane of Apple Lane is entitled to take possession of the Thane’s House and associated buildings (barn and corral). The two-story stone house has been neglected for four years and is need of repairs and renovation but is in good structural shape.

Approximately 50 acres of apple orchards (equivalent to five hides) have been allocated for the support of the thane and their household. The orchards are divided into five locations (each about ten acres), each tended and harvested by a tenant family that takes half of the harvest and remitting the other to the thane. With decent management, the orchard generates about 200 L a year for the support of the thane as described in the RuneQuest rules.

The ransom price for a thane is 1,000 L, paid by the tribe.

### Responsibilities
The thane is the representative of the tribal king in the Apple Lane area, and tasked with the defense of Apple Lane, its residents, and local travelers. The thane is also tasked with maintaining the local peace, serving as the local judge, and assisting the local clans in tribal matters.
The thane is also expected to participate regularly in Orlanth cult activities and must serve as a lay member of Orlanth Rex.

### Tenants of the Thane
There are five tenant families working the orchards. They reside outside of the hamlet, near the orchards, in small rectangular stone cottages with an acre or less of tilled land.
Some of the tenants are potentially usable as a local militia—all possess at least a spear, shield, and battle axe.
Skills of 50+% are listed: if required, the gamemaster can determine these as 50+1D20%.

#### Tenant Family 1
Good farmers and proud Orlanthi, three children.
- Rastolf: Adventurous, stubborn, proud, former rebel. Skills: Farm, Herd.
- Serla: Shrewd, superstitious, ambitious. Skills: Farm, Herd.

#### Tenant Family 2
Mediocre farmers, former scribes who left the Jonstown Library for mysterious reasons, no children.
- Ivo Stone Brook: Lay member of Lhankor Mhy, bitter, idealistic. Skills: Farm, Read/Write Theyalan.
- Muriah: Lay member of Lhankor Mhy, tolerant, pragmatic. Skills: Farm, Read/Write Theyalan.

#### Tenant Family 3
The Rotroots are poor farmers; they survive through the generosity of their Varmandi kin and from thieving. They are surprisingly loyal to their patron.
- “Ma” Rotroot: Cagey matriarch, opportunistic, manipulative. Skills: Farm, Bargain.
- Vigil: Big, bullying, thieving. Skills: Herd, 2H Spear.
- Phineas: Violent, lazy, charming. Skills: Fast Talk, Battle Axe.

#### Tenant Family 4
Good farmers, competent fighters. Caleb was outlawed for seven years for murder, but survived and returned to find his wife and son waiting for him, three children.
- Caleb the Black: Brooding, former outlaw, loyal to family. Skills: Farm, Herd, Shortsword, Medium Shield.
- Abrela: Hardworking, determined, skilled woodsman. Skills: Farm, Herd, Battle Axe.

#### Tenant Family 5
Good farmer, skilled fighter. Kalla is not Orlanthi and is said to worship his spear.
- Kalla the Foreigner: A hardworking solitary farmer, Kalla is secretly a former Lunar soldier from the Heartlands who deserted. Skills: Farm, Speak New Pelorian, 1H Spear, Large Shield.

## Tusk Rider Skills and Magic
The Glorantha Bestiary contains a writeup for the Cult of the Bloody Tusk, to which the Tusk Riders in this adventure belong.
The following are summaries of a skill and Rune magic taught by that cult.

#### Bloody Cut (10) (Manipulation Skill)
This skill is used to dispatch animals and captured prisoners, causing the victim great trauma and pain so that its spirit may be trapped within a part of its body. The Bloody Cut is often combined with the Death Binding spell but is also practiced on its own.

#### Appease Earth (Rune spell)
1 Point

Ceremony

This ritual is used to perform sacrifices to the Bloody Tusk, through either a sacrifice of grain or other food fed to tuskers; blood sacrifice (preferably human sacrifice); or sacrifice of magic items or treasure of great worth. When cast successfully (see the Glorantha Bestiary for specifics), one characteristic point of the supplicant’s tusker mount is increased by +1 (chosen by the rider). No characteristic may be increased upon the normal maximum by this means.

#### Death Binding (Rune spell)

1 Point

Ceremony

When combined with Bloody Cut and a sacrifice of 1 POW, the victim’s spirit is trapped and bound within its tail (or hand, if humanoid). The caster can use the victim’s magic points to cast spells and can cast the victim’s spirit magic spells, if any. This magic is only usable by the one that trapped the spirit and is broken if a proper burial ceremony (such as Peaceful Cut or Prepare Corpse) is intoned over the tail or hand, or if it is destroyed.


#### Pain Tooth (Rune spell)

2 Points

Ranged, Temporal, Nonstackable

When cast on a weapon, this spell causes any hit that penetrates armor to incapacitate the location struck, in addition to the weapon’s normal damage. The victim can recover from this incapacitation by rolling under their CON×2 and can try to do so each round. If a target has been injured in more than one location by a Pain Tooth, they need only roll CON×2 a single time to be freed of all incapacitating effects.

#### Seal Wound (Rune spell)

2 Points

Ranged, Temporal, Nonstackable

Damage done by the weapon this spell is applied to will not regenerate or heal magically but must be healed in the normal time it would take. Thus Healing 2 will cauterize a wound, but not heal it, Healing 6 will restore a limb but not mend it, etc.


# Cattle Raid
In this scenario, the adventurers are recruited to stop a pair of saber-toothed cats that have been threatening the sacred cattle of a local clan. While defending the herd, they come into confrontation with warriors from a rival clan who threaten to take the cattle. How the adventurers handle this confrontation determines whether there will be a bloody feud between two clans or even tribal war.

## Background
The Varmandi are a small clan of the Colymar tribe of around 450 members. Their lands are near Apple Lane.
Their main settlement is Oakton, a small walled village surrounding the Oak of Vengeance (which is the clan’s cult sanctuary). They have a long history of bloody feuds, especially with the Orleving clan of the Malani tribe. The Varmandi are notorious for having started several tribal wars with their feuds.

The clan has comparatively little arable land—only about 60 hides worth. Most of the clan’s wealth is in the form of their livestock. Some of the animals are needed for plowing and daily support, but the Varmandi keep about 400 cattle and 1200 sheep in the hills. Of these, some 100 cattle belong to the tribal Orlanth temple (whose high priest is the tribal king) and are sacred to the local Orlanth cult. The livestock graze primarily in the Gejay Hills, which often puts them in conflict with the neighboring Malani tribe, specifically the Orleving clan.

The Varmandi suffered terribly during the Lunar Occupation, and after the previous chieftain was killed in Starbrow’s Rebellion, the women of the Varmandi chose Gentle Vastyr to lead the clan away from war. Vastyr died during the Great Winter, and in 1622, the Wind Lord Korol Serpent-Tongue became clan chief. Since then, the clan has taken increasingly violent actions to aggressively defend their interests.

### Current Events
This scenario is assumed to take place in early Dark Season 1625, less than a season after __Defending Apple Lane__, although gamemasters are free to set it at whatever date works best for their campaign. Kallyr Starbrow has captured the city of Jonstown and repelled a last-ditch Lunar counterattack at the Battle of Dangerford. The Colymar did not participate in either of those events.

The situation in Dragon Pass is still very tumultuous but Sartar is fully freed from the Lunar Empire. Old rivalries between clans and tribes are quickly reemerging to the fore now that the Lunar threat has receded.

Early Dark Season is the onset of winter and the first smattering of snow has just fallen. This is when the fabled clearwine grapes are harvested near Clearwine Fort to make ice wine. Livestock are taken from the hilltops into the valleys and ravines to get fodder.

## Starting the Adventure

Several ways to start the adventure are presented below. If one of the adventurers is the Thane of Apple Lane, then looking after the sacred herd is simply one of the duties of the office. If the adventurers are members of a local clan or are members of the Colymar tribe, they might just be told to defend the sacred cattle by the appropriate authorities.

Alternately, the adventurers might simply be hired to defend the herd by the owner of the Tin Inn in Apple Lane.

### Notes on livestock
In the average Heortling clan of 1200 people, there are about 200 hides worth of productive wealth (both land and livestock). In Sartar, about two-thirds of that wealth is agricultural, and about one-third is livestock beyond that necessary to work the land.

The average clan owns about 1000 cattle, with some 250 being kept on the individual farmsteads year-round (to help with plowing, milk, etc.), and about 750 being herded into the hills each spring, only to return with the coming of winter. There are also about 2000 sheep, which are driven many kilometers (often more than a day away from the clan’s lands) in search of pasture. Not all these cattle belong to the clan; most clans also are responsible for a herd of cattle belonging to the tribal Orlanth temple (whose high priest is the tribal king).

On average, one herder, assisted by a trained shadowcat, can drive 20 cattle or 50 sheep. Pasturing herds are usually about 100 animals.

These large pastoral herds are owned principally by temples, particularly by the local Orlanth temple. Cattle are primarily consumed in cult sacrificial feasts. The tribal Orlanth temple maintains the king’s household and cult activities with their own herds that are then cared for by the clans of the tribe. The tribal herds range in size from just a few hundred cattle (in the case of a small tribe like the Amad or Tovtaros) to more than 2000 cattle (such as the Colymar). Each clan is responsible for some part of the tribal livestock and receives much of the benefit of the herd (primarily milk and some of the offspring). However, they must provide the temple with renders of dairy products, as well as provide cattle for cult sacrifice.

### Option One: The Thane of Apple Lane

The Thane of Apple Lane is the representative of the tribal king in the region and can be expected to defend the sacred herd. The tribal queen summons the adventurer to feast at Clearwine Fort and holds an audience with the adventurer at the Palace of the Kings– a sign of respect, honor, and loyalty!
The adventurer eats good roasted meat, drinks clearwine out of a communal kylix, and is even given a silver spiral bracelet worth 50 L. Afterwards, the queen tells the adventurers that a pair of monsters have been preying on the sacred cattle herd cared for by the Varmandi clan. She asks them to travel to the Varmandi lands and kill the monsters. She then summons a young Varmandi herder named Heortarl to act as their guide.

### Option Two: For Clan, Cult, or Tribe
Adventurers from the Colymar tribe may be asked to defend the sacred herd by a tribal, cult, or clan leader, especially if they have a Passion of 70% or more with an appropriate leader or community. Some options based on the highest appropriate Passion possessed by one or more adventurers: Loyalty (Colymar Tribe) or Loyalty (Queen Leika): Like the Thane of Apple Lane option, the tribal queen summons the adventurer to feast at Clearwine Fort and holds an audience with the adventurer at the Palace of the Kings—a sign of respect, honor, and loyalty! The adventurer eats good roasted meat, drinks clearwine out of a communal kylix, and is even given a silver spiral bracelet worth 50 L. Afterwards, the queen tells the adventurer that a pair of monsters have been preying on the sacred cattle herd cared for by the Varmandi clan. She asks them to travel to the Varmandi lands and kill the monsters. She then summons a young Varmandi herder named Heortarl to act as their guide.

- Loyalty (clan): Adventurers with a high Loyalty
Passion to one of the clans of the Colymar tribe are asked by their chief to aid the Varmandi clan by killing the monsters that threaten the sacred cattle herd. The chief emphasizes ties of marriage and/or cult with the Varmandi, as well as the opportunity to demonstrate the superiority of the adventurers’ clan over the others. The chief arranges for a young Varmandi herder named Heortarl to act as their guide.
- Loyalty (Orlanth or Ernalda temple):
Adventurers with a high Loyalty Passion to their Orlanth or Ernalda temple are told by their priests (Asborn Thriceborn for Orlanth; Ereneva Chan for Ernalda) that a pair of monsters has been preying on the sacred cattle herd cared for by the Varmandi clan. The adventurers are each offered 1 point of spirit magic from their temple if they complete the task. The priests have arranged for a young Varmandi herder named Heortarl to act as their guide.
- Loyalty (Sartar): Dunorl the Old of Clearwine
Fort asks to meet with adventurers with a high Loyalty Sartar. Dunorl warns them that despite being free from the Lunar Occupation, Sartar is in a more precarious position than it has been since Starbrow’s Rebellion. The tribes are fighting amongst each other and it will take very little to start a tribal war that might quickly end the kingdom’s hard-won independence before Prince Kallyr has time to put Sartar on a more secure foundation. Dunorl is concerned that the monsters preying on the sacred cattle herd cared for by the Varmandi clan is just the sort of problem that might trigger a war between the Colymar and Malani tribes that would destroy Sartar. He asks them to kill the monsters that threaten the sacred cattle herd and has arranged for a young Varmandi herder named Heortarl to act as their guide.

### Option Three: Monster-Hunters for hire

At the Tin Inn, Brightflower is looking for adventurers to hunt a pair of monsters that have been preying on the Varmandi herds. She is particularly concerned about the threat to the “sacred herd” belonging to the tribal Orlanth temple. Brightflower offers to pay 50 L per monster killed and reminds the adventurers that friendship with the Varmandi clan is quite a reward on its own. She can be bargained up to 100 L per monster (success = 60 L, special success = 75 L, critical success = 100 L).

### Questions and Answers
Regardless of how the adventurers are recruited, intelligent adventurers should have some questions. Here are some answers to some likely questions.

#### Q: What kind of monsters are these?
A: “Terrible giant cats with long, deadly fangs called sakkars.
They are said to be incarnations of Death. They feed on anything from cattle to dinosaurs. These monsters are deadly.
They have already killed four cattle and seriously wounded one of the local herders.”

#### Q: Where were the monsters last seen?
A: “The better part of a day north of here, in the Gejay Hills.
Heortarl can take you there—he knows all the herder trails.”

#### Q: Are there any other monsters that might be causing trouble?
A: “There’s plenty of bandits in these hills. Tusk Riders, Orlevings, Lunar deserters... there was even a troll gang here a decade ago. But bandits we can kill—few are willing to hunt down a sakkar.”

#### Q: Why can’t the Varmandi deal with these monsters?
A: “The Varmandi are a small clan and all their warriors are required to protect them from bandits and to keep the Malani and others at bay. They don’t possess spare warriors to hunt down sakkars as well!

#### Q: Who are the Orlevings?
A: “The Orlevings are part of the Malani tribe of Two-Ridge Farm. They have a long history of feuds and raids with the Varmandi clan and there is no love lost between the two.” Once the adventurers are satisfied, a young Varmandi herder named Heortarl is summoned to guide the adventurers into the Gejay Hills to find the monsters and protect the cattle.

## Into the Gejay Hills
Heortarl brings the adventurers to the pastures where the monsters were last spotted. Heortarl takes the adventurers along herders’ trails through the rocky hills and mixed woods of pine, oak, and grassland. At this time of year, there is likely some snow on the ground, and there is more snow expected in later weeks. Heortarl wears a winter cloak made of antelope hide.

The gamemaster should strive to make Heortarl helpful and likeable to the adventurers. That shouldn’t be hard: Heortarl is enthusiastic and good-hearted; he is full of questions about the adventurers and their deeds. Heortarl wishes he could be an adventurer, fighting battles and going to far-away lands, instead of just herding someone else’s cows.

Old ruins are scattered about in the hills. Sheep graze alongside the remnants of the stone foundations of a building, a campfire is made behind a broken wall, a pile of ancient masonry, and so on. If the adventurers ask Heortarl about them, he doesn’t know much but he is happy to talk about them or anything else, despite his general lack of knowledge.

#### Q: What are these ruins?
Heortarl: “I dunno. There’s plenty of them around here.
The elders say that the Wyrmsfriends used to live here. The ruins probably belonged to them.”

#### Q: What do you know about sakkars?
Heortarl: “They are like monstrous shadowcats with swords for fangs. They hunt cows, sheep—even people!”

#### Q: What do you know about these hills?
Heortarl: “We’ve been in these hills since Time began. I’ve seen the Rainbow Mounds and the Giant’s Table. I heard that a terrible troll outlaw once lived there, preying on folk!
I’ve even seen the Old Grave where some dead god slumbers.
There’s lots of good pasture, cool streams with shady trees, and friendly spirits.”

If the adventurers ask Heortarl about bandits, the young man warns them about the perfidious Orleving clan.
“They are all thieves and murdering bandits! Did you know that Orlev Eye-spear stole the goddess from Iskalli son of Varmand, back when our ancestors first came to these lands?” If asked, Heortarl admits he doesn’t know what that really means, but he assures the adventurers that whatever it means, it can’t be a good thing.

### Heortarl
Initiate of Orlanth Adventurous.

Male, age 16. Varmandi clan.

Heortarl is a young man, only recently initiated into Orlanth Adventurous and adulthood. He is curious, enthusiastic, and good-hearted—even towards most strangers. His hatred of the Orleving is an exception—Heortarl grew up on tales of how Orlev Eye-spear stole the goddess from Iskalli Varmandsson and other foul deeds, but the main reason for his hatred is that an Orleving Lunar auxiliary killed Heortarl’s father during Starbrow’s Rebellion.

##### Characteristics
- STR 11
- CON 13
- SIZ 12
- INT 13
- DEX 10
- POW 14
- CHA 13
- Hit Points: 13
- Move: 8
- DEX SR: 3
- SIZ SR: 2
- Runes: Air 70%, Movement 70%.
- Rune Points: 1 (Orlanth Adventurous)
- Rune Spells: Shield.
- Spirit Magic: Heal 1, Mobility.
- Magic Points: 14
- Passions: Hate (Lunar Empire) 60%, Hate (Orleving Clan) 80%, Loyalty (Varmandi Clan) 60%.

## The Herders
After about six hours of travel, the adventurers can see a herd of about 100 black cattle being herded by five herders (Vargast, Jareena, Varlanth, Hengall, and Cara). They are rounding up the cattle for the evening. All carry slings and javelins; none wear armor beyond thick vests. The cattle are marked with the Black Spear of Colymar– this is the sacred herd of the Orlanth temple. A campfire provides welcome warmth.

The herders welcome Heortarl and the adventurers with some food (flat bread, cheese, and dried meat) and some wine that has been watered down (unless one of the adventurers manages to befriend Vargast, who has a skin of unadulterated clearwine). The herders are glad that the adventurers are here, as the clan is responsible for the sacred herd. If any of these cattle are killed or stolen, not only does the clan lose those resources but they must also make up the loss to the tribe or run the risk of angering the tribal gods!

The herders are concerned about the sakkars that have been preying on their herds, but also worried about the presence of Orleving bandits. The gamemaster should use this as an opportunity for the Varmandi to emphasize how much they hate the Orleving. The herders swap tales of betrayal, thievery, and murder—the cause of all attributed to the Orlevings.

During the night, the herders take shifts watching: adventurers who take a shift are accepted as fast friends; those who don’t are considered mere mercenaries.

Nothing happens during the night, although the gamemaster may introduce some suspense by letting adventurers who succeed with a Listen roll hear loud roars (like those of a lion) some distance away (perhaps a few kilometers).

### Varmandi Herders
These herders are typical for Dragon Pass. These herders are herding cattle, but they could just as easily be herding sheep. They attack as skirmishers using their slings. They fight in melee only if they cannot retreat. They all wear quilted linen jerkins, calf-hide trews, and fur-lined hooded cow-hide cloaks as protection against the cold.

#### Vargast

Initiate of Orlanth Adventurous.

Male, age 25. Varmandi clan.

Vargast is the most experienced herder in the group. He is cautious and suspicious of outsiders; once his trust is earned, he is a loyal friend. He is a surprising connoisseur of local food and wines—and always carries a small stash of local delicacies. Vargast is in love with an unmarried woman from the Ernaldori clan. Vargast is protective of his younger brother Varlanth.

## Monsters!
The next day, the herders round up the cattle to move them to new pastures. The herd travels slowly over a couple of hills, grazing contentedly.
Ask the players if their adventurers are wearing armor as they travel. Assuming the adventure takes place in Dark Season, emphasize how cold and uncomfortable it is. The gamemaster may wish to impose penalties on Perception skills for adventurers who insist on wearing bronze or iron armor everywhere, anywhere ranging from –10% for composite helms to –35% for full helms. The penalties to Move Quietly skills are described on the Armor chart on page 215 of the RuneQuest core rules.
Jareena warns the adventurers, “Be vigilant here. We are near Orleving lands.” With a successful Homeland Lore roll, the adventurers know that they are already on lands generally recognized as belonging to the Orleving. Alternately, a successful Scan roll spots a boundary stone.
If confronted, the herders respond proudly, “We don’t recognize those claims. This is Varmandi land.” Really attentive adventurers may try to Search or Track for signs of the sakkars. On a special or critical success with Search or a successful Track, they can see relatively recent signs of sakkars in the vicinity. However, there is not enough spoor to follow the sakkar—just enough to know that have recently been in the area.
By mid-afternoon, they arrive in a narrow lightly wooded valley cut by a small stream. There are plenty of ponds to water the animals. An adventurer who says they are trying to scout may make an opposed roll of their Scan against the Hide skill of the saber-toothed cats (80%). If the adventurer wins the contest, they get one melee round warning before the saber-tooth cats pounce!

The cats attack the herd by charging out of their concealed position and each springing at one of the cattle, stabbing with its bite and then digging in with its massive claws. By the end of the round, two cattle are down on the ground— either dead or bleeding to death.
The rest of the herd scatters and stampede in several directions. Adventurers who want to engage in melee with the saber-toothed cats need to succeed on a DEX×5 roll or else they get charged by a stampeding animal. A stampeding animal will attack and then continue running away.
The saber-toothed cats try to flee if attacked. They attack humans only if they are cornered, badly wounded, or otherwise desperate. They can be tracked to where they retreat to—a rocky outcrop. If followed there and attacked, the cats will fight to the death.

### Saber-toothed Cats
This is a mated pair of saber-toothed cats. The male is a massive creature, larger than average. The female is slightly smaller than average. They do not hunt as some lions do, where the male allows the female to do most of the dirty work. Here, each cat pulls its own weight.
Saber-toothed cats are particularly vicious in combat.
Each cat gets two claw attacks and one bite attack per round, all against the same opponent. If the cat hits a victim with both front paws, it will continue to hold on, ripping with its hind legs from that point on, and continuing to bite until the victim dies.

Vargast and Jareena are trying to round up the largest subherd, while Cara tries to apply First Aid to Hengall (who was injured by the stampeding cattle). Jareena tells the adventurers, “Go help Varlanth and Heortarl! They followed the herd that went north towards Ormsthane Vale—that’s the Orleving farming lands!”

## Raiders
The herd of some 50 cattle has come to stop near where the narrow valley opens to join the broad Ormthane Vale.
A successful Scan or Listen roll reveals that an armed band of a half-dozen warriors are nearby—a failure results in the adventurer noticing the band only once they have come within javelin range. Two of the warriors are mounted, the rest are on foot. The warriors are marked as members of the Orleving clan.
The exact number depends on the number of adventurers:

If there are only three or fewer adventurers, then there should only be Deseros, Erlanda, and Alantos. If there are four adventurers, then add Arene. If there are more than four adventurers, add Urandorl and Intamorl.

The gamemaster should feel free to tailor the number of warriors to the capabilities of the party. The warriors should be approximately an equal match for the adventurers. One of the mounted warriors, an armored thane named Deseros Bloodsword, demands that the Varmandi run away and leave the cattle here. Deseros warns that if they try to stay or try to take the cattle, "I will take you in fetters and ransom you back to your kin."

Ask the players what they plan to do. Unless they act quickly and decisively, events will overtake them. Some options they might choose:

### Option One: Negotiate With the Orlevings
The adventurers may try to negotiate with Deseros. If the adventurers offer him 25 or more cattle, he will be satisfied and will let the herders take the cattle back to Oakton. The adventurers can try to Bargain with Deseros. With a successful Bargain, Deseros will accept twenty cattle. With a special success in Bargain, he will accept only fifteen cattle. On a critical success, he is willing to take only ten cattle. On a failure, he will demand thirty cattle. On a fumble, he will demand the entire herd. If the adventurers agree to give cattle to Deseros, Heortarl will be outraged and demand that they revoke the deal. "You cannot give Orlanth's cattle to those thieves and betrayers!"

The adventurers must overcome Heortarl's Hate (Orleving Clan) Passion using an appropriate Rune or skill (such as Orate). If they fail, Heortarl shouts abuse at the Orleving, triggering the same result as if they did nothing (see Option Three: Ignore Deseros). If the adventurers manage to overcome Heortarl's Hate (Orleving Clan) Passion, they may return to Oakton with whatever cattle they don't give to Deseros. However, upon their return, they must justify their decision to the Varmandi clan leaders and may find themselves on the hook for repaying the lost cattle to the tribal king.

### Option Two: Attack
The adventurers may decide to attack the Orleving warriors. Heortarl and Varlanth will aid the adventurers. The Orleving are unwilling to fight to the death over cattle. If any take serious wounds (such as reducing any hit location to negative hit points or reducing total hit points to three or less), they will retreat to the Orleving lands to seek reinforcements. By evening, an Orleving war party numbering a dozen mounted and armored warriors will show up. The following day, they make a search through the Gejay Hills—how determined their search is depends on whether any of the Orleving were killed in the attack.

### Option Three: Ignore Deseros
If the adventurers ignore Deseros and try to drive the cattle back, Deseros demands that they stop and gathers his warriors into a skirmish line. Deseros then threatens them unless they stop; however, he takes no action except to follow them. His followers bang their weapons on shields, and shout threats, but also take no action. Heortarl says to the adventurers, "They are afraid of you!" He then mocks the Orlevings as cowards and says, "We are taking our cattle home and there's nothing you impotent whoresons can do about it!" At this point, Alantos the Huge loses his temper and throws a javelin at Heortarl, doing 9 points of damage to his abdomen, incapacitating (and nearly killing) Heortarl. The next round Deseros leads an attack on the adventurers, hoping to chase them off. As above, they are unwilling to fight to the death over cattle.

## Orleving Raiders

These warriors are led by Deseros Bloodsword, a Malani thane, and are competent warriors. He is careful and cautious, if not particularly innovative, in his tactics. He is reluctant to start a fight with a comparable-strength group of adventurers and prefers negotiation; once combat is engaged, however, he and his followers fight like wildcats.

## Reward and Reputation
The adventurers get +1D3 points of Reputation each for rescuing the sacred herd; +1D3 Reputation each if they chased off the Orleving warriors (regardless of whether they started a feud with the Orleving clan in the process). If the adventurers manage to return to Oakton with the sacred herd largely intact and the Varmandi herders alive, they prove their friendship with the Varmandi clan. Chief Korol presents them with a small herd of ten cows from the clan's own herd and swears friendship with the adventurers. If the adventurers return with a much-diminished sacred herd and two or more Varmandi herders dead, they will meet a cool but civil reception. It should become obvious to them that the chief suspects that their efforts were halfhearted or even deliberately ineffectual, and he will begrudgingly reward them a single cow as a token gesture.

## Revenge and Retaliation
If the adventurers kill any of the Orleving, the Orleving demand compensation or seek vengeance upon the Varmandi and adventurers. The Orleving demand that full ransom price for all dead Orleving and half the ransom price for all wounded Orleving be paid by the adventurers and/or the Varmandi clan. Soon after, when the adventurers are in Apple Lane, an Issaries Herald named Yandara Truespeaker arrives, prominently displaying her Herald's Staff (which indicates she is under the protection of Issaries and the Lightbringers). Yandara has been paid by the Orleving to deliver their demands for compensation for the killed and injured kin. The adventurers are given a season to pay up or the Orleving shall seek revenge against them and their kin. The extent of the Orleving retaliation is up to the gamemaster, but it should initially be roughly proportionate to the ransoms of the Orlevings injured or killed by the adventurers. Orleving warriors might start by attacking tenants or other low-status associates of the adventurers and escalate based on the adventurers' actions. A feud can make an excellent component in an ongoing campaign, with tit for tat potentially escalating into war.

## Return to Oakton
If the adventurers negotiate with Deseros and manage to overcome Heortarl's Hate (Orleving Clan) Passion, they can return to Oakton with whatever cattle they don't give to Deseros. However, upon their return, they must justify their decision to the Varmandi clan leaders (requiring successful use of appropriate Communication skills) or may find themselves on the hook for repaying the lost cattle to the tribal king.

# The Dragon of Thunder Hills
This scenario has the adventurers confront one of the most fearsome monsters of Glorantha—the dream dragon. A clever party has plenty of opportunities to arm themselves with weapons and magic that will give them an edge against the dream dragon; however, a reckless or foolhardy party will likely meet their demise at the claws and breath of Yerezum Storn. Success against the dragon marks the adventurers as truly heroic material and dramatically enhances their wealth and Reputation.

This dream dragon was worshiped as an incarnation of a regional goddess by the previous inhabitants during the era of the Third Council (also called the Empire of the Wyrms Friends) but vanished nearly six centuries ago.
She is one of the many draconic entities awakened because of the Dragonrise and emerged from the Queen’s Tomb not long ago.

## Current Events
This scenario is assumed to take place in Fertility Week of Storm Season 1625, although as always gamemasters are welcome to set the scenario whenever works best for their campaign. This is the snowiest time of the year, and there might be as much as 2D20 cm of snow on the ground. The temperature is hovering around freezing, and the snow can quickly turn into rain and then back again to snow. Winter thunderstorms are not uncommon.

The situation in Dragon Pass has stabilized somewhat.
The Lunar Army has retreated to its winter quarters in AldaChur. In Boldhome, Kallyr Starbrow has been recognized as Prince of Sartar by all the tribes, including even Queen Leika (whose acknowledgement of Kallyr was notably reluctant and guarded).

## Dragon Raid!
The adventurers are traveling south, through the snow along the herder’s path between Apple Lane and the Nymie Vale.
They are headed to Clearwine Fort. To the east, the thick woods of Tarndisi’s Grove can be seen; to the west looms the high cliffs of the Starfire Ridges.

The gamemaster should encourage the players to discuss why their adventurers are traveling to Clearwine Fort.
Perhaps they are headed there for seasonal worship or to bring some matter up with the tribal leaders.

Once the players have eased into their roles, the gamemaster should ask for Listen skill rolls. Those adventurers who succeed hear a loud roar off in the distance.

The gamemaster should then ask for Scan rolls.
Adventurers who heard the roar get a +25% bonus to their Scan skill. Those who succeed see the ominous outline of a winged serpent with four limbs rising high into the air above the nearby Nymie Vale, a cultivated area of small farms and vineyards.

If they all fail their rolls, they are surprised as the dragon flies directly over them, terrifying their mounts (mounted characters should make a Ride roll to avoid being thrown).
The dragon spirals around, then flies towards the Thunder Hills.

The adventurers should be encouraged to check out where the dragon came from—the dragon may have preyed on a local settlement or stead! The adventurers may check against their Loyalty (Colymar Tribe) or Honor Passion to be motivated to check out what happened.

### Jenstal’s Stead
A short run towards the valley brings the adventurers to Jenstal’s Stead, a small collection of farmhouses and other buildings. Fields and vineyards surround the buildings.
Jenstal is a member of the Anmangarn clan. If any of the adventurers are of the Colymar tribe, Jenstal is related to them by marriage (the gamemaster should just come up with whatever works best if there is more than one adventurer clan).

- Animal Corral: Low stonewalls surround this corral. Scattered within are the corpses of six partially eaten cattle and the mangled corpses of two humans. Here Jenstal and Hulik died trying to defend the livestock from the dragon.
- Barn: The corpse of a mangled ox and a human (Berke) partially block the entrance to the barn. Within the barn are the intact and undamaged corpses of two cows and one ox—Berke managed to get these animals into the barn before they succumbed to the dragon’s poisonous breath.
- Inner Hearth: In the inner hearth are the surviving members of Jenstal’s family: Arndala, Korostal, Penene the Younger, Arnstal, and Eona. The corpse of Penene the Elder (Jenstal’s mother) is here as well (she earlier succumbed to the dragon’s poisonous breath).

When the adventurers arrive, Penene and Eona are tending to Arndala and Korostal, both of whom are badly wounded. Arnstal huddles in a corner. Penene pleads, “Can you help my mother and uncle? They are badly hurt and not moving, although I do not think they are dead. Where is father and Hulik? Did they escape? Please, help us!” If the adventurers tell of the fate of Jenstal and Hulik, Penene and Arnstal break down in tears. Arndala weakly says, “There will be time enough for vengeance later. The tribe must be told. Please, take my children to Clearwine Fort and tell the queen what happened here!”
